  \section*{Summary}
  \addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Summary}

  Mixed holomorphic-topological string theory on
  \(\R^2_{\mathrm{top}}\times\C^2_{\mathrm{hol}}\) has, at each formal
  holomorphic-symplectic brane point, a closed sector that coincides
  with the chiral Hochschild cohomology of the boundary algebra; this
  manuscript computes the formal-Darboux coordinate expression of that
  coincidence at \(N\) Dirac branes.

  \medskip\noindent
  The B-model topological string is governed at all loops by the
  Kodaira--Spencer master action of
  Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa \cite{bcov} in the renormalized
  Batalin--Vilkovisky (BV) form of Costello and Costello--Li
  \cite{costello-renormalization,costello-li-quantum-bcov,costello-li-open-closed-bcov}.
  Fix a holomorphic-symplectic brane point in a Calabi--Yau target
  and an admissible protected Hamiltonian restriction of the ambient
  closed-open theory.  The local model is the holomorphic-topological
  BF theory on
  \(X=\R^2_{t,s}\times\C^2_{z_1,z_2}\), topological in \((t,s)\) and
  holomorphic in \((z_1,z_2)\): the open sector is a stack of \(N\)
  Dirac branes on the worldline \(\R_t\); the closed sector is the
  shifted-cotangent local Lie algebra
  \(\mathfrak g=\mathfrak h\ltimes\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1]\)
  on \(\widehat{\C^2}_0\), \(\mathfrak h=\C[[z_1,z_2]]/\C\cdot 1\);
  the brane measurement is the trace functional
  \(J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}\,f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}\).
  Existence of an admissible protected restriction for a given
  compact target is not proved here; it is an external input,
  controlled across volumes by the matched-conventions theorem
  \ref{thm:matched-conventions-transfer}.

  \medskip\noindent
  \emph{The classical BV theory.}  The BV fields are an
  $\mathfrak h$-connection $\mathcal A$ and antifield $\mathcal B$,
  \[
    \mathcal A\in
    \Omega^\bullet(\R^2)\widehat\otimes\Omega^{0,\bullet}(\C^2)
    \widehat\otimes\mathfrak h,
    \qquad
    \mathcal B\in
    \Omega^\bullet(\R^2)\widehat\otimes\Omega^{0,\bullet}(\C^2)
    \widehat\otimes\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1],
  \]
  with $(-1)$-shifted symplectic pairing
  $\int_X\langle\mathcal B,\mathcal A\rangle$ given by integration of
  mixed-HT forms against the holomorphic volume $dz_1\,dz_2$ and the
  residue pairing
  $\langle f,\omega\rangle=\operatorname{Res}_0\,f\omega$ between
  $\mathfrak h$ and $\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}$.  The classical
  action is the BF action
  \[
    S=\int_X\langle\mathcal B,
      (d+\bar\partial)\mathcal A
      +\tfrac12[\mathcal A,\mathcal A]_{\mathfrak h}\rangle,
    \qquad
    Q=d+\bar\partial+d_{\mathrm{CE}},\qquad
    \{S,S\}=0.
  \]
  The total classical interaction is
  $S=S_{\mathrm{free}}+I$ with kinetic term
  $S_{\mathrm{free}}=\int_X\langle\mathcal B,(d+\bar\partial)\mathcal A\rangle$
  and interaction
  $I=\tfrac12\int_X\langle\mathcal B,[\mathcal A,\mathcal A]_{\mathfrak h}\rangle$.
  Gauge fixing $Q^{\mathrm{GF}}=d^*+\bar\partial^*$ for a flat metric
  on \(\R^2\) and a Hermitian metric on \(\C^2\) gives the
  generalized Laplacian \(D=[Q,Q^{\mathrm{GF}}]\), symmetric heat
  kernel $K_t=e^{-tD}$, and propagator
  \(P_{\varepsilon,L}=\int_\varepsilon^L\tfrac12(Q^{\mathrm{GF}}\!\otimes\!1
    +1\!\otimes\!Q^{\mathrm{GF}})\,K_t\,dt\).  The Costello effective
  interaction at scale \(L\) is \(I[L]=W(P_{\varepsilon,L},I)\); the
  quantum master equation (QME)
  \[
    Q I[L]+\tfrac12\{I[L],I[L]\}_L+\hbar\Delta_L\,I[L]=0
  \]
  is the constraint on the quantization, and its failure in the
  reduced non-scalar lane is the class
  \([\operatorname{Ob}^{\mathrm{red}}_{w,\partial,\hbar}]\)
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:app-theta-three-finite-window-H1}).  Costello
  deformation cohomology of \(I[L]\) is the obstruction theory of the
  closed-to-open coupling \(\Phi_N\)
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:closed-to-open-derived-centre-map}).

  \medskip\noindent
  \emph{Tate setup and the residue pairing.}  Throughout, $\mathfrak h$
  carries the linear topology defined by the images of
  $\mathfrak m^k\subset A$, with continuous dual
  $\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}=
   \mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{cts}}_\C(\mathfrak h,\C)$ in the category of
  Tate $\C$-vector spaces (linearly compact / pro-finite).  The
  residue pairing identifies
  $\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}$ with the Matlis principal-part
  module
  \[
    \mathcal P=H^2_{\mathfrak m}(A)\otimes\omega_A
     =\bigoplus_{a+b>0}\C\,\rho_{a,b},
    \qquad
    \omega_A=dz_1\wedge dz_2,
    \qquad
    \rho_{a,b}=z_1^{-a-1}z_2^{-b-1}\,dz_1\,dz_2,
  \]
  the holomorphic-surface analog of the Beilinson--Drinfeld
  Heisenberg current on the formal disk.  Inverse limits over Matlis windows
  \(\mathcal P_{\le N}=\bigoplus_{a+b\le N}\C\rho_{a,b}\) are read as
  derived limits \(\mathbf{R}\!\varprojlim_N\mathcal P_{\le N}\),
  with $\varprojlim^1$-data tracked.

  \medskip\noindent
  \emph{The brane.}  A stack of \(N\) Dirac branes is the boundary
  condition along \(\R_t\subset X\) pinning
  \(z_i\mapsto\phi_i\in\mathfrak{gl}_N\):
  \[
    \rho_\phi\colon A\longrightarrow\End(\C^N),
    \qquad z_i\longmapsto\phi_i,
  \]
  with worldline kinetic term
  \(\int_{\R_t}\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_1\dot\phi_2)\) and Koszul
  antifield $\psi\in\mathfrak{gl}_N[1]$,
  $Q\phi_i=0$, $Q\psi=[\phi_1,\phi_2]$, realizing the conjugation
  moment-map ideal as the derived zero fibre; the matrix entries of
  \([\phi_1,\phi_2]\) form a non-regular sequence for \(N\ge 2\), so
  the open ghost-zero classical brane moduli is the derived
  commuting-pair stack
  \([\mu^{-1}_{\mathrm{der}}(0)/GL_N]\), and the Hilbert scheme
  \(\Hilb^N(\C^2)\) in its Nakajima ADHM-quiver presentation appears only
  on the cyclic framed stable branch where one has adjoined a framing
  vector \(v\in\C^N\) with \(\C[\phi_1,\phi_2]\cdot v=\C^N\).  The
  Moyal/Weyl deformation of the brane Poisson algebra restricts to
  the noncommutative Hilbert scheme of Nekrasov--Schwarz on that
  cyclic framed stable branch.

  \medskip\noindent
  \emph{The brane measurement \(J\).}  A Hamiltonian
  \(f\in\mathfrak h\) is measured on the brane as the gauge-invariant
  trace character
  \[
    J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}\,f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}.
  \]
  \(J\) is the unique linear \(GL_N\)-equivariant primitive
  single-trace measurement
  \(\mathfrak h\to\mathcal O\bigl(\mu^{-1}_{\mathrm{der}}(0)\bigr)^{GL_N}\)
  that is Darboux-natural under the formal symplectomorphism group,
  Dirac-descended through the moment-map ideal, scalar-reduced, and
  linear-normalized \(J(z_i)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}\phi_i}\); its
  value at a fixed representation is the cyclic trace, a scalar
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:Jf-coordinate-uniqueness}).  The forcing is
  monomial-by-monomial through Procesi--Razmyslov, adjacent-swap
  exactness on the moment-map zero fibre, and
  Loday--Quillen--Tsygan single-trace primitivity
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:no-natural-correction}).  Among admissible
  protected Hamiltonian sectors at a holomorphic-symplectic brane
  point, \(J\) is the terminal datum
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:formal-local-universal-property}).

  \medskip\noindent
  \emph{Closed-open coupling.}  The bulk factorization algebra of
  classical observables admits two equivalent presentations: a
  Costello--Gwilliam holomorphic factorization algebra on \(\C^2\)
  valued in \(E_2\)-algebras
  \[
    \mathcal A^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{bulk}}(B)=
    \mathcal F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{Ham}}(D^2\times B),
    \qquad
    \mathcal F^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{Ham}}=
    C^{\mathrm{CE}}_\bullet\bigl(
      \Omega^\bullet_c(U)\widehat\otimes
      \Omega^{0,\bullet}_c(B)\widehat\otimes\mathfrak g\bigr),
  \]
  with \(C^{\mathrm{CE}}_\bullet\) the Chevalley--Eilenberg (CE) chain
  functor for a dg Lie algebra,
  in the BV / heat-kernel analytic framework of
  Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~II
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:mixed-HT-factorization-theorem},
  Corollary~\ref{cor:E2-bulk-algebra}); equivalently, in the
  Beilinson--Drinfeld algebraic-geometric framework, a factorizable
  \(\mathcal D\)-module on \(\mathrm{Ran}(\C^2)\) valued in \(E_2\)-algebras,
  the holomorphic-2-dimensional analog of a chiral algebra.  The
  passage between the two presentations is the de Rham complex of
  the \(\mathcal D\)-module (Riemann--Hilbert).  The brane is the
  $E_1$-module
  \(A^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\partial,N}=
   C^\bullet_{\mathrm{CE}}(\mathfrak{gl}_N,\mathrm{Kosz}_N)\),
  with $\mathrm{Kosz}_N=\mathrm{Sym}\bigl(\mathfrak{gl}_N\oplus
  \mathfrak{gl}_N\oplus\mathfrak{gl}_N[1]\bigr)$ and Koszul
  differential $Q\psi=[\phi_1,\phi_2]$, on a small interval of the
  worldline (Definition~\ref{def:E1-brane-algebra}).  A Hamiltonian
  $f\in\mathfrak h$ has two CE coordinate avatars on
  $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak h\ltimes\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1]$:
  the generator-dual $c_f$ acts on observables as the Hamiltonian
  Schouten field $\theta_f$, and the cotangent-dual $u_f$ is measured
  on the brane as $O_f=J(f)$.  The reduced closed-to-open dictionary
  on these generators,
  $c_f\mapsto\theta_f$, $u_f\mapsto O_f=J(f)$
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:closed-to-open-derived-centre-map}), is the
  generator-level part of a putative chain map
  \[
    \Phi_N\colon\mathcal A^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{bulk},0}
    \longrightarrow Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{E_1}\!\bigl(A^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\partial,N}\bigr)
  \]
  into the derived $E_1$-centre of the brane $E_1$-algebra
  identified by Lurie--Francis additivity; the chain-level lift is
  conditional on vanishing of the obstruction vector
  $\operatorname{Ob}_{\mathrm{cent/QME/desc}}$
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:app-unreduced-cotangent-lift-obstruction}) with
  chosen null-homotopies.  The native
  bulk datum is two-complex-dimensional: a holomorphic factorization
  algebra on \(\C^2\), or factorizable \(\mathcal D\)-module on
  \(\mathrm{Ran}(\C^2)\), valued in \(E_2\)-algebras --- a curve vertex
  algebra is its restriction to a one-dimensional reduction
  (Remark~\ref{rmk:not-a-curve-voa}).

  \medskip\noindent
  \emph{Capelli scalar anomaly.}  Closed Hamiltonian reduction
  quotients the constant Hamiltonian line $\C\cdot 1$, on which
  $\operatorname{Tr}\,1=N$; the brane retains it as the trace-$U(1)$
  centre-of-mass cocycle of $N$ coincident D-branes on noncommutative
  $\C^2_\hbar$.  The class
  $\hbar N[\bar c]\in H^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\bar A,\C)[[\hbar]]$,
  $\bar A=\C[z_1,z_2]/\C\cdot 1$, has four
  equivalent realizations: the Lie 2-cocycle
  $\omega(f,g)=[\{f,g\}]_0$ on $\bar A$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:omega-cocycle}),
  the unreduced Poisson bracket
  $\{\operatorname{Tr}\phi_1,\operatorname{Tr}\phi_2\}=N$, the Moyal
  commutator
  $[\Phi_\hbar(z_1),\Phi_\hbar(z_2)]_\star=\hbar N\cdot I$ in
  $M_N(\C)$, and the trace-$U(1)$ central charge of the noncommutative
  D-brane stack on $\C^2_\hbar$
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:app-scalar-anomaly-transgression}).  The strict
  scalar-reduced Hamiltonian action upgrades to a projective action
  on the Capelli central extension
  \(0\to\C\to\mathfrak h_{\hbar,N}\to\bar A\to 0\).

  \medskip\noindent
  \emph{Obstruction calculus.}  Write \(\Theta_{\mathrm{loc}}\) for
  the local trace-substitution datum
  \((J,\rho_\phi,\psi,c_f\mapsto\theta_f,u_f\mapsto J(f))\) packaged
  as a degree-one element of the filtered \(L_\infty\)-algebra
  \(\mathfrak K_{\mathrm{loc}}\) of
  Definition~\ref{ssec:master-deformation-target}.  Globalization,
  quantization, all-bidegree Weyl/Moyal compatibility, and chain-level
  upgrades are filtered \(L_\infty\) transports of
  \(\Theta_{\mathrm{loc}}\) into a target deformation complex
  \(\mathfrak K_{\mathcal T}\), with master equation curvature
  \(F_{\mathcal T}(\Theta_{\mathrm{loc}}+\tau)=0\)
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:obstruction-twist-dichotomy}).  All-bidegree
  Weyl/trace compatibility is controlled by the radial Weyl cokernel
  \(\Omega^{\mathrm{rad}}_{a,b}\in\operatorname{coker}B_{a,b}\) on
  the decorated PBW Stokes operator \(B_{a,b}\)
  (Theorems~\ref{thm:componentwise-quantum-coefficient-comparison},
  \ref{thm:app-radial-bidegree-rational-reduction}), with
  \(\Omega^{\mathrm{rad}}_{a,b}=0\) for all \((a,b)\) conditional on
  the Harish-Chandra/Levasseur--Stafford radial-image input
  Statement~\ref{stmt:app-radial-external-input}; the chain-level
  \(P_0\)-factorization-centre lift of \(\Phi_N\) by the
  factorization-centre vector
  \(\operatorname{Ob}_{\mathrm{cent/QME/desc}}\)
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:app-unreduced-cotangent-lift-obstruction}); the
  reduced non-scalar Costello QME by the class
  \([\operatorname{Ob}^{\mathrm{red}}_{w,\partial,\hbar}]\in
  H^1\bigl(\mathfrak Q^\bullet_{w,\partial,\hbar}(I),\,
  d_{\mathrm{QME}}\bigr)\) in the filtered weighted brane-defect
  local-functional complex, whose order-three CE-source row
  \(\theta_3\) gives the prototype
  \(H^1_{\mathrm{bare}}=\C\!\cdot\!\mathsf E_{\theta_3,M}\) killed by
  the unique counterterm
  \(d_M C_{\theta_3,M}=-\mathsf E_{\theta_3,M}\), \(c=1\), in any
  finite window \(M\ni\theta_3\)
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:app-theta-three-finite-window-H1}); and the
  strict all-window Bochner--Martinelli current transfer by
  \(\operatorname{Ob}^\Pi_{\mathrm{BM}}\), nonzero on every finite
  Matlis window \(\mathcal P_{\le N}\)
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:bmk-finite-window-no-go}), with coherent action
  on the pro-Matlis substituted target
  \(\widehat{\mathcal P}_\Pi=\varprojlim_N\mathcal P_{\le N}\)
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:promatlis-universal-property}).  These four
  classes are the obstructions identified for the upgrade directions
  considered here; they sit in the filtered \(L_\infty\) algebra
  \(\mathfrak K_{\mathrm{loc}}\) governing deformations of
  \(\Phi_N\).  We do not assert that the minimal \(L_\infty\)-model
  has exactly these four nonzero higher brackets; that would require
  a separate minimal-model classification.

  \medskip\noindent
  The local model is the two-complex-dimensional analog of the
  Heisenberg chiral algebra of Beilinson--Drinfeld, with
  \(\mathcal P\) playing the role of the Kac--Moody central pairing
  module \(\Omega_K\) for a curve \(K\) and \(\mathfrak h\) that of
  regular functions; it is the formal local sector of the Costello
  twisted M-theory family \cite{costello-twistedM}; and on its
  cyclic framed stable branch the universal formal-Darboux stalk chart
  of \(\Hilb^N(\C^2)\) in its noncommutative Moyal deformation.

  \medskip\noindent
  \emph{External targets.}  Globalization to compact Calabi--Yau
  targets ---BCOV holomorphic anomaly, Gopakumar--Vafa,
  Donaldson--Thomas--- transports \(\Theta_{\mathrm{loc}}\) into the
  corresponding compact-target deformation complex.  The formal-Darboux
  stalk theorem produced here, combined with descent, the QME, an
  anomaly match, and a locality verification, is the candidate input;
  no compact-target theorem is asserted here, and cross-volume use is
  routed through the matched-conventions theorem
  \ref{thm:matched-conventions-transfer}.

  \medskip\noindent
  The local theorem is the formal-Darboux stalk chart restriction of
  the derived chiral centre \(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)\)
  (the chiral Hochschild cochain centre of a chiral algebra \(A\)),
  constructed by ordered bar-cobar duality and modular Maurer--Cartan
  calculus, identified with the bulk-boundary datum of a 3d
  holomorphic--topological boundary-bulk system, and sourced from a
  Calabi--Yau category through the two-stage functor
  \(\Phi_d=\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}\circ\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d\)
  (factorization-algebra functor \(\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d\) of a
  Calabi--Yau \(d\)-category followed by Lurie--Ayala--Francis
  factorization-homology specialization
  \(\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}(\mathcal F)
  =\int_{\Sigma_{d-1}}\mathcal F|_C\)),
  \(A=\Phi_d(\mathcal C)\).  Conventions match the global theorem
  through the obstruction class
  \(\operatorname{Ob}_{\mathrm{UKD}}\) of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:matched-conventions-transfer}.

\subsection*{Conventions and notation for the local theorem}\label{ssec:binding-convention-layer}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{Conventions and notation for the local theorem}

The convention layer below binds throughout the body, the appendices,
and every cross-volume statement; deviation requires an explicit
matched-conventions theorem
(Theorem~\ref{thm:matched-conventions-transfer},
Appendix~\ref{app:convention-contract}).

\paragraph{Geometric and algebraic conventions.}
The complex target dimension is \(d=\dim_\C X\); the manuscript proves
the formal-Darboux stalk at the Dirac brane stack on
\(\R^2_{\mathrm{top}}\times\widehat{\C^2}_{0,\mathrm{hol}}\), with
\(d=\dim_\C\widehat{\C^2}_0=2\).  The local geometry is
\(\R^2_{\mathrm{top}}\times\C^2_{\mathrm{hol}}\) at a brane:
the only Calabi--Yau datum is the trivial holomorphic symplectic
\(\Omega_{\mathrm{loc}}=dz_1\wedge dz_2\) on \(\widehat{\C^2}_0\), used
for the BV pairing, divergence-free Hamiltonian fields, and cyclic
trace densities of Definition~\ref{def:app-sign-hamiltonian-cyclic}; it
is not a compact CY\(_3\) assumption, not a BCOV theorem, not a license
to import quintic / OSV / GV / MNOP / Abel--Jacobi / CoHA / Igusa / BKM
material into the core local theorem surface
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cross-volume-firewall}).
The native bulk object is the holomorphic
\(E_2\) / factorization algebra on \(\C^2\),
\(\Omega^{0,\bullet}_c(B)\,\widehat\otimes\,\mathfrak g\), with shifted-cotangent
BF Lie algebra
\(\mathfrak g=\mathfrak h\ltimes\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1]\),
\(\mathfrak h=\C[[z_1,z_2]]/\C\cdot 1\)
(\S\ref{ssec:hamiltonian-bf-algebra},
Theorem~\ref{thm:bf-action-uniqueness-qme}).
Curve vertex operator algebra (VOA), Zhu algebra, and Vol II
\(\C\times\R\) chiral-topological theorems enter only after a
controlled reduction with explicit \(z_2\)-mode or principal-part data,
pushed-forward bracket, BV pairing, brane image, and anomaly matching;
a curve VOA is a one-dimensional restriction of the native
two-complex-dimensional factorization algebra, not the native object
(Remark~\ref{rmk:not-a-curve-voa}).
The worldsheet \(\Sigma\) is a complex curve, possibly with boundary
or marked points; framing on \(S^3\) is stated when invoked, with
deviation flagged.

\paragraph{Sign, degree, and renormalization conventions.}
BV degree, ghost number, and form degree are tracked separately,
none identified with another: BV degree is cohomological, ghost
number is the antifield/ghost grading of the classical fields
\((\phi_1,\phi_2,\psi)\) and \((\mathcal A,\mathcal B)\), and form
degree is the de Rham/Dolbeault exterior grading on
\(\Omega^\bullet(\R^2)\widehat\otimes\Omega^{0,\bullet}(\C^2)\).
Koszul, Chevalley--Eilenberg, polyvector, and Gerstenhaber bracket
signs are fixed in
Appendix~\ref{app:sign-conventions}, with cyclic trace conventions
fixed in Definition~\ref{def:app-sign-hamiltonian-cyclic} and the
CE/PV cochain comparison signs in Lemma~\ref{lem:app-sign-ce-pv}.
The propagator weight in heat-kernel renormalization is the explicit
Costello \(\bigl(K_L-K_\epsilon\bigr)\) interval kernel of
\cite{costello-renormalization}, recorded in
Appendix~\ref{sec:app-factorization-current-conventions}.
The QFT loop parameter \(\hbar\) and the string-genus parameter
\(g_s\) are distinguished at every reference: \(\hbar\) controls the
local BV / Moyal expansion of the matrix Koszul algebra, while \(g_s\)
controls the string genus expansion of the ambient closed-open
theory.  The BCOV holomorphic-anomaly equation enters with the
BCOV-strict sign of \cite{bcov} preserved by the matched-conventions
divergence row of Theorem~\ref{thm:divergence-bcov}; the local theorem
of this manuscript does not assert the holomorphic-anomaly equation,
only its compatibility with the local convention layer.

\paragraph{Homological taxonomy.}
Hochschild homology \(HH_\bullet\), cyclic homology, and
negative-cyclic homology \(HC^-_\bullet\) are distinguished at every
reference (Appendix~\ref{app:sign-conventions}): the cyclic trace
symbol \(\operatorname{Tr}\) is the graded cyclic quotient, the
Hochschild trace is its image under the Connes \(B\)-operator, and
the negative-cyclic class is the constellation-level Calabi--Yau
pairing of Vol III.  Drinfeld centre \(Z(\mathcal C)\), derived
centre \(Z^{\mathrm{der}}\), and chiral derived centre
\(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}\) are distinguished: the bar
construction \(\mathrm{Bar}(A)\) is the twisting coalgebra and is not
the bulk; the bulk is
\(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)\simeq C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}(A,A)\),
the chiral Hochschild cochain centre.

\paragraph{Constellation-anchored objects.}
The boundary open category on the Kato--Nakayama log boundary is
\(\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}_\partial=\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d(\mathcal A)\big|_{\partial X^{\mathrm{KN}}}\);
factorization-homology specialization is
\(\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ch}}_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}(\mathcal F)
   =\int_{\Sigma_{d-1}}\mathcal F\big|_C\)
(Lurie--Ayala--Francis).  The Dirac brane stack at \(N\) branes is
the derived commuting variety
\([\mu^{-1}_{\mathrm{der}}(0)/\mathrm{GL}_N]\)
with \(Q\psi=[\phi_1,\phi_2]\), \(Q\phi_i=0\); the brane reading is
the physical realization.  The Capelli scalar identification
\(\hbar N[\bar c]=\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}|_{J(f)}\) is the
projective curvature evaluation at the trace generator
(Theorem~\ref{thm:capelli-omega-central-identification}).  The
modular line bundle on
\(\overline{\mathcal A}_g\) coincides with \(\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}\)
at level \(g\); the Igusa cusp form \(\Phi_{10}\) is its level-2
section, and \(\Delta_5=\Phi_{10}^{1/2}\) is a level-2 modular section,
not a compact BPS Hilbert space.  The CE/PV dictionary
\(c_f\mapsto\theta_f\), \(u_f\mapsto J(f)\) of
Lemma~\ref{lem:app-sign-ce-pv} is the Koszul resolution of
\(C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_b,A_b)\) at the brane vacuum
(Theorem~\ref{thm:formal-local-universal-property}).

\paragraph{Theorem-control predicates.}
Every theorem in the manuscript is stated within the following
control surface:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Native \(\C^2\) holomorphic \(E_2\) / factorization-algebra
  taxonomy is retained before any curve-chiral reduction; a curve VOA
  result is invoked only after a controlled reduction with
  pushed-forward bracket, brane image, and anomaly matching.
\item BMK lane: the one-pair analytic pro-Matlis retract of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:promatlis-universal-property} is not a strict
  native all-window support-local current transfer; the obstruction
  on every fixed finite Matlis window is
  \(\operatorname{Ob}^\Pi_{\mathrm{BM}}\)
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:bmk-finite-window-no-go}), and the pro-tower
  \(\widehat{\mathcal P}_\Pi=\varprojlim_N\mathcal P_{\le N}\) is the
  supremum form selected by that obstruction.
\item Larger non-scalar \(\theta_3\) row is evidence only with
  one of: a Chevalley--Eilenberg ancestor, a scalar-zero Costello
  local counterterm, or a complete companion-face table
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:app-theta-three-finite-window-H1}); tower
  compatibility passes through
  \(\Delta^1_{M,N}=-\pi_{M,N}\mathfrak b^M+\mathfrak b^N\) together
  with the secondary \(\varprojlim^1 H^0\) primitive class.
\item The radial / Weyl theorem surface is
  \(\Omega^{\mathrm{rad}}_{a,b}\in\operatorname{coker}B_{a,b}\),
  equivalently the decorated PBW Stokes obstruction for
  \(D^\square_{a,b}=C^+_{a,b}\partial_2\), with failure exactly a
  signed row \((\phi,-\lambda)\in\ker B^*_{a,b}\) such that
  \(\lambda(E^+_{a,b})-\phi(T_{a,b})\ne 0\)
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:app-radial-bidegree-rational-reduction}).
\item Larger non-scalar Costello / QME theorem requires the data
  tuple: filtered scalar projection \(\widehat\sigma_{\mathrm{sc}}\),
  finite row arrays, primitive matrix \(A^M c=-r^M\), transition
  matrices, Roos compatibility, centrality homotopies, and the
  curved bulk-to-defect kernel
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:app-unreduced-cotangent-lift-obstruction}).
\item The brane-preserving \(\Omega\)-background uses the normal
  scaling on \(N_L X=\R_s\oplus\C_{z_1}\oplus\C_{z_2}\) with the
  brane time \(t\) fixed, torus
  \(T_\Omega=\C^*_{\varepsilon_s}\times\C^*_{\varepsilon_1}\times\C^*_{\varepsilon_2}\),
  twisted differential
  \(Q_\Omega=Q+\iota_{V_\Omega}\) with \(Q_\Omega^2=L_{V_\Omega}\),
  inverted normal weights, residue-vs-Euler normalization, and
  stratified factorization data; literal \((t,s)\)-rotation does
  not preserve the brane line and is not native unless a different
  fixed-locus problem is defined.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Cross-volume firewall.}  No compact, global, modular,
automorphic, specialization, framing, descent, virtual-cycle,
attractor, or operadic structure of the seven external programmes
(BCOV / Kodaira--Spencer; Vol III CY-to-chiral; Igusa / BKM;
GW / DT / PT / MNOP; OSV; mixed-Dunn / six-relation; global
Hamiltonian descent on a compact holomorphic-symplectic surface)
follows from the local theorem
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cross-volume-firewall}); cross-volume use is
governed by the matched-conventions theorem
(Theorem~\ref{thm:matched-conventions-transfer}) and the universal
Koszul-descent acceptance class
\(\operatorname{Ob}_{\mathrm{UKD}}(\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{tar}})\),
with a nonzero coordinate the precise mechanism that prevents
acceptance.

\section{Introduction}

\subsection{The Dirac brane formal-stalk chart in one calculation}\label{ssec:formal-darboux-stalk-one-calculation}\label{ssec:brane-microscope-one-calculation}

A Hamiltonian \(f\in\C[[z_1,z_2]]\) on the formal symplectic normal
polydisk is read on the brane stack as a single
\(GL_N\)-invariant number, the trace character
\(J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}\).  The supporting
geometry is the product
\[
  X=\R_t\times\R_s\times\C^2_{z_1,z_2},
\]
formally completed at the origin of \(\C^2\), each factor carrying
one ingredient of the calculation: the worldline \(\R_t\) carries the
open-line observable algebra, the transverse direction \(\R_s\)
carries the bulk-to-boundary propagator, and the formal
holomorphic-symplectic normal disk \(\widehat{\C^2}_0\) carries the
Hamiltonian algebra.  Relaxing the formal completion admits global
geometry into the brane stalk.

A stack of \(N\) Dirac branes on
\(L=\R_t\times\{s=z_1=z_2=0\}\subset X=\R_t\times\R_s\times\C^2\)
is a Chan--Paton matrix substitution
\[
  \rho_\phi\colon A=\C[[z_1,z_2]]\longrightarrow\End(\C^N),
  \qquad z_i\longmapsto\phi_i\in\mathfrak{gl}_N,
\]
an algebra homomorphism modulo the Dirac ideal
\([\phi_1,\phi_2]=0\) imposed below.  The holomorphic symplectic form
\(dz_1\wedge dz_2\) pulls back along this substitution and traces to
\[
  \theta_N=\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_1\,d\phi_2),\qquad
  \Omega_N=\operatorname{Tr}(d\phi_1\wedge d\phi_2),
\]
and \(GL_N\)-conjugation acts symplectically with moment map
\(\mu(\epsilon)=-\operatorname{Tr}\epsilon[\phi_1,\phi_2]\), so Dirac
reduction imposes the first-class constraint
\([\phi_1,\phi_2]=0\): \(\rho_\phi\) factors through the commuting
variety, and the simultaneous spectrum of \((\phi_1,\phi_2)\) is the
configuration of \(N\) points in the polydisk.  A Hamiltonian
\(f\in A\) is measured on the brane as the gauge-invariant trace
character of its representation,
\[
  J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}\,\rho_\phi(f)}
       =\overline{\operatorname{Tr}\,f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}
       \in A_\partial=\widehat{\mathbf S}_{\mathrm{Tate}}(\mathfrak h),
       \qquad
       \mathfrak h=A/\C\cdot 1.
\]
The brane represents the commutative coordinate ring \(A\); the
Hamiltonian Lie algebra \(\mathfrak h\) acts on the representation
moduli \(\Rep_N(A)/\!/GL_N\) by Hamiltonian vector fields.

For polynomial \(f\in A_{\mathrm{poly}}=\C[z_1,z_2]\) the substitution
\(f(\phi_1,\phi_2)\in\End(V)\) is defined on the commuting variety
\(\Rep_N(A_{\mathrm{poly}})=\{(\phi_1,\phi_2)\in\mathfrak{gl}_N^2
:[\phi_1,\phi_2]=0\}\) and gives the polynomial measurement
\(J_{\mathrm{poly}}\colon A_{\mathrm{poly}}/\C\cdot1\to
\mathcal O(\Rep_N(A_{\mathrm{poly}}))^{GL_N}\).
For formal \(f\in A=\C[[z_1,z_2]]\) the substitution lives on the
formal representation functor
\[
  \Rep_N^{\wedge}(A)(R)=\bigl\{(\phi_1,\phi_2)\in(\mathfrak m_R\otimes\End V)^2
  :[\phi_1,\phi_2]=0\bigr\},
  \qquad R\text{ Artin local over }\C,
\]
on which every formal power series in \(\phi_1,\phi_2\) terminates
modulo \(\mathfrak m_R^k\); the formal measurement is
\(J\colon\mathfrak h\to
\mathcal O(\Rep_N^{\wedge}(A))^{GL_N}\), and the Tate completion
\(A_\partial=\widehat{\mathbf S}_{\mathrm{Tate}}(\mathfrak h)\)
records its image at the universal Artin base.  The polynomial
commuting variety \(\Rep_N(A_{\mathrm{poly}})\subset\mathfrak{gl}_N^2\)
is the uncompleted algebraic model; the formal functor
\(\Rep_N^{\wedge}(A)\) is its formal completion at the closed point
\(\{\phi_1=\phi_2=0\}\) inside the commuting variety, and on polynomial
Hamiltonians the two evaluations agree.

An odd matrix antifield \(\psi\in\mathfrak{gl}_N[1]\) and BV
differential \(Q\) with \(Q\phi_i=0,\ Q\psi=[\phi_1,\phi_2]\) realize
the moment-map ideal as the Koszul derived zero fibre, and the
matrix entries of \([\phi_1,\phi_2]\) are not a regular sequence for
\(N\ge2\): the open sector is the derived zero fibre, not an
ordinary smooth quotient
(\S\ref{ssec:open-mixed-bft}).  The closed BV sector takes its
fields in the shifted-cotangent extension
\[
  \mathfrak g=\mathfrak h\ltimes\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1].
\]
A Hamiltonian \(f\in\mathfrak h\) carries two Chevalley--Eilenberg (CE)
coordinates on \(\mathfrak g\): the generator-dual \(c_f\) acting as
the Hamiltonian Schouten polyvector (PV) field \(\theta_f\) on the
representation moduli, and the cotangent-dual \(u_f\) measured on the
brane as the boundary observable \(O_f=J(f)\); the CE/PV cochain
dictionary on generators is
\[
  c_f\longmapsto\theta_f,\qquad u_f\longmapsto O_f
\]
(Theorem~\ref{thm:reduced-ce-pv-central-operation}).  The residue
pairing realizes the cotangent module
\(\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}\) as the Grothendieck--Matlis
principal-part module
\[
  \mathcal P
   =\bigoplus_{a+b>0}\C\rho_{a,b},
   \qquad
   \rho_{a,b}=z_1^{-a-1}z_2^{-b-1}\,dz_1\,dz_2,
\]
with Taylor Hamiltonians paired by residue
(Theorem~\ref{thm:principal-part-coadjoint-uniqueness}); residues are
defect momenta conjugate to sampling, distinct from the polynomial
PBW shadows of the open one-\(\psi\) sector.

\begin{ex}[Single-point evaluation, \(N=1\)]\label{exa:point-probe-N1}
  For \(N=1\) the matrices commute automatically, and
  \(J(f)=f(\phi_1,\phi_2)\) is the evaluation map
  \(\C[[z_1,z_2]]\to\C\) at the point
  \((\phi_1,\phi_2)\in\C^2\).  The brane is one point of the polydisk;
  Procesi--Razmyslov degenerates to the polynomial ring on two scalar
  variables, and \(J\) is the classical evaluation homomorphism.
\end{ex}

\begin{ex}[Linear Hamiltonians and the scalar anomaly]\label{exa:linear-hamiltonians-anomaly}
  For \(f=z_1\), \(g=z_2\), the unreduced brane Poisson bracket is
  \[
    \{\operatorname{Tr}\phi_1,\operatorname{Tr}\phi_2\}=N.
  \]
  This scalar is the open-side image of the closed--open mismatch
  Lie 2-cocycle \(\bar c\in Z^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\bar A,\C)\), the
  cohomology class \([\bar c]\in H^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\bar A,\C)\)
  attached to the constant Hamiltonian line that vanishes closed-side
  and survives open-side as the brane centre of mass
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:omega-cocycle},
  Theorem~\ref{thm:u1-center-anomaly}).  Moyal quantization with
  Weyl-symmetric quantization map \(\Phi_\hbar\) and Kontsevich--Moyal
  star \(\star\) returns the same scalar at order \(\hbar\),
  \([\Phi_\hbar(z_1),\Phi_\hbar(z_2)]_\star=\hbar N\cdot I\) in $M_N(\C)$; the first
  scalar-reduced \(\mathfrak{gl}_N\) quantum-master-equation
  obstruction is the same class shifted by \(\hbar\),
  \(W_1^{\mathrm{sc,ord}}=\hbar N[\bar c]\)
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:app-scalar-anomaly-transgression}).
\end{ex}

\begin{ex}[A single Matlis residue]\label{exa:single-matlis-residue}
  The Grothendieck--Matlis principal-part
  \[
    \rho_{a,b}=z_1^{-a-1}z_2^{-b-1}\,dz_1\,dz_2
      \in\mathcal P\subset H^2_{\mathfrak m}(\C[[z_1,z_2]])\,dz_1dz_2
  \]
  in the local-cohomology module
  \(H^2_{\mathfrak m}=H^2_{\{0\}}\) at the maximal ideal
  \(\mathfrak m=(z_1,z_2)\) pairs with \(f\in\mathfrak h\) by
  \(\langle\rho_{a,b},f\rangle=\operatorname{Res}_0(\rho_{a,b}f)\) and
  detects the Taylor coefficient of \(z_1^az_2^b\) in \(f\).  The
  residue-coadjoint formula
  \(z_1^pz_2^q\cdot\rho_{a,b}=-(pb-qa+p-q)\rho_{a-p+1,b-q+1}\) of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:principal-part-coadjoint-uniqueness} acts on
  \(\mathcal P\) without local nilpotency.  A polynomial PBW class
  attached to the same \((a,b)\)-bidegree exists in the open
  one-\(\psi\) sector with a strictly different \(\mathfrak h\)-module
  structure
  (Corollary~\ref{cor:descendant-coadjoint-difference}); the residue
  mode here and that polynomial class are separated by the
  Fourier-polarized Rees bridge.
\end{ex}

\subsection{The Dirac brane}

The primitive open object is the open category
\(\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}_\partial\) on the Kato--Nakayama log
boundary; a chosen vacuum \(b\) gives the chart
\(A_b=\End(b)\), and at the stack of \(N\) Dirac branes
\(A_b\) is the matrix endomorphism algebra of the corresponding
boundary state.  The manuscript works in this chart, not in the
primitive.

A holomorphic Hamiltonian \(f\in\C[[z_1,z_2]]\) on the formal
symplectic polydisk \((\widehat{\C^2}_0,dz_1\wedge dz_2)\) is
infinite-dimensional.  A finite-rank reduction exists exactly when the
closed polydisk is paired with an open boundary that names the two
normal coordinates: a stack of \(N\) Dirac branes on the topological
line
\[
  L=\R_t\times\{s=z_1=z_2=0\}
  \subset
  X=\R_t\times\R_s\times\C^2_{z_1,z_2}
\]
realises the Dirac brane stack as the derived commuting variety stack
\([\mu^{-1}_{\mathrm{der}}(0)/GL_N]\) with moment map
\(\mu(\phi_1,\phi_2)=[\phi_1,\phi_2]\) on the
\(\lie{gl}_N\)-valued Chan--Paton pair
\((\phi_1,\phi_2)\in\lie{gl}_N\oplus\lie{gl}_N\)
(Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi shifted symplectic structures
\cite{ptvv-shifted-symplectic}; the brane reading is
Calaque \cite{calaque-derived-branes}; the BV/Koszul realisation of
the moment-map zero fibre is
Costello~\cite{costello-renormalization}*{Ch.~5}).
A Hamiltonian \(f\) admits the literal trace evaluation
\[
  J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}
\]
on the chart, the formal Darboux coordinate of the chiral Hochschild
cohomology at the brane vacuum.  The data are the formal
Darboux polydisk at the brane point and the Chan--Paton substitution
\[
  z_1\rightsquigarrow\phi_1,\qquad z_2\rightsquigarrow\phi_2,\qquad
  \phi_i\in\mathfrak{gl}_N .
\]
Every other piece of open-side structure---symplectic potential,
moment map, Dirac constraint, BV differential, gauge-invariant operator
algebra---follows from this substitution.

\paragraph{The substitution forces the open BV theory.}
The pullback of \(dz_1\wedge dz_2\) along the substitution, traced over
Chan--Paton, is
\[
  \Omega_N=\operatorname{Tr}(d\phi_1\wedge d\phi_2),\qquad
  \theta_N=\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_1\,d\phi_2).
\]
\(GL_N\)-conjugation acts symplectically with moment map
\[
  \mu(\epsilon)=-\operatorname{Tr}(\epsilon[\phi_1,\phi_2]),\qquad
  \epsilon\in\mathfrak{gl}_N,
\]
so the first-class constraint is \([\phi_1,\phi_2]=0\): the locus on
which the Chan--Paton pair commutes and the \(N\)
branes sit at \(N\) points of the polydisk.  With multiplier
\(A\in\Omega^1(\R)\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_N\) the Dirac first-order action is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:open-action-derived}
S_\partial
  =\int_\R\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(\phi_1\,d\phi_2+A[\phi_1,\phi_2]\bigr),
\end{equation}
and the constraint is realized by the Koszul derived zero-fibre model
for the moment-map equation~\cite{costello-renormalization}*{Ch.~5}.
Introduce a fresh odd matrix-valued
antifield
\(\psi\in\mathfrak{gl}_N[1]\) --- the BV antifield \(\psi=A^*\) of the
Lagrange multiplier \(A\), independent of \(\phi_1,\phi_2\) and not a
function of them --- and let \(Q\) be the unique derivation defined on
generators by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:open-Q}
Q\phi_1=Q\phi_2=0,\qquad Q\psi=[\phi_1,\phi_2].
\end{equation}
The displayed equation is the Koszul resolution of \(\mu^{-1}(0)\) on
the BV side: every matrix entry of the commutator equals
\(Q\psi^i{}_j\) and is therefore \(Q\)-exact, so the moment-map ideal
vanishes in cohomology.  The
matrix entries of \([\phi_1,\phi_2]\) are not a regular sequence in
\(\C[\phi_1{}^i{}_j,\phi_2{}^i{}_j]\) for \(N\geq2\); the construction
is the derived zero fibre, not a regular Koszul resolution.  Its
ghost-zero invariant cohomology
\[
  \mathcal O\bigl([\mu^{-1}_{\mathrm{der}}(0)/GL_N]\bigr)
    =H^0\bigl((\mathbf S^\bullet\lie{gl}_N^{\oplus2}
       \otimes\Lambda^\bullet\lie{gl}_N[1])^{GL_N},Q\bigr)
\]
identifies the structure sheaf of the derived commuting variety stack
with the \(GL_N\)-invariant cohomology of the Koszul complex; the
degree-zero classical invariant ring of the commuting-pair quotient
is the bottom row of this identification.  The degree-zero
Hamiltonian trace comparison ignores higher Koszul homology; that
homology enters only through explicit invocation.  The
ghost-zero invariant ring is
\[
  \mathcal R_N
    =\C[\phi_1{}^i{}_j,\phi_2{}^i{}_j]\otimes\Lambda(\psi^i{}_j),
  \qquad
  H^0(\mathcal R_N^{GL_N},Q).
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:open-action-reduction} produces \eqref{eq:open-action-derived}
from the shifted open Chern--Simons coefficients;
Lemma~\ref{lem:dirac-probe-reduction} verifies the moment-map content;
Proposition~\ref{prop:open-bv-truncation} verifies that ghost-zero
truncation is consistent.  None of \eqref{eq:open-action-derived},
\eqref{eq:open-Q}, or \(\mathcal R_N^{GL_N}\) is independent input.

\paragraph{Word-ordering independence of \(J\).}
\(J(f)\) depends only on the symbol \(f\), not on the chosen word
ordering: adjacent letters in any trace word commute up to a
\(Q\)-exact correction,
\[
  Q\,\operatorname{Tr}(\psi\,v\,u)=\operatorname{Tr}([\phi_1,\phi_2]\,v\,u),
\]
so a trace adjacent-swap is the BRST image of an antifield insertion.
Cyclic invariance and iterated \(Q\)-exact swaps reduce every cyclic
word in \(\phi_1,\phi_2\) to a symmetric symbol \(z_1^kz_2^l\): the
trace family \(\{J(f)\}\) is the connected single-trace primitive
content of the Chan--Paton pair, the full stable
\(GL_N\)-invariant ring is the polynomial algebra on this primitive
(Procesi--Razmyslov first fundamental theorem), and the closed sector
\(\bar A=\C[z_1,z_2]/\C\cdot 1\) acts on the brane through \(J\)
alone.

\begin{lem}[Trace realisation of the formal polydisk]\label{lem:formal-stalk-trace}\label{lem:probe-trace-microscope}
  In the stable connected ghost-zero open observables
  \(H^0(\mathcal R_N^{GL_N},Q)\), the substitution map
  \[
    J\colon\C[z_1,z_2]\longrightarrow H^0(\mathcal R_N^{GL_N},Q)\big|_{\mathrm{stable\ conn.}},
    \qquad
    f\longmapsto\overline{\operatorname{Tr}f(\phi_1,\phi_2)},
  \]
  is surjective with kernel exactly the constant line
  \(\C\cdot 1\subset\C[z_1,z_2]\).  Procesi--Razmyslov first
  fundamental theorem identifies the full stable \(GL_N\)-invariant
  ring with the polynomial algebra on the trace family
  \(\{J(f):f\in\bar A\}\), so every gauge-invariant operator is a
  polynomial in trace observables and the closed sector
  \(\bar A=\C[z_1,z_2]/\C\cdot 1\) acts on the brane through
  \(J\) alone.
\end{lem}

\begin{proof}
  Procesi--Razmyslov~\cites{procesi,razmyslov} identifies
  \(\C[\mathfrak{gl}_N\oplus\mathfrak{gl}_N]^{GL_N}\) with the algebra
  generated by traces of noncommutative monomials in two letters.
  Cyclic invariance equates traces of cyclically equivalent words; the
  \(Q\)-exactness of \eqref{eq:open-Q} equates traces differing by one
  adjacent swap.  Iterating, every cyclic word
  \(\operatorname{Tr}(w(\phi_1,\phi_2))\) reduces to a symmetric symbol
  \(\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_1^k\phi_2^l)=J(z_1^kz_2^l)\) in
  \(H^0(\mathcal R_N^{GL_N},Q)\).  Stable connected linear independence
  over \(N\) of these symmetric symbols is standard.  The constant
  Hamiltonian substitutes to \(N\cdot I\), a scalar multiple of the
  identity, killed by passage to the connected stable class.
\end{proof}

The substitution-realised Lie algebra image of \(\bar A\) inside the
open trace cohomology generates the open sector; it is not a
representation of \(\bar A\) on a chosen module.

\begin{ex}[\(N=1\): the geometric prototype]\label{ex:N1-commuting-case}
  At \(N=1\), \([\phi_1,\phi_2]=0\) holds tautologically, so the Dirac
  constraint imposes nothing and the antifield \(\psi\) is decoupled.
  The Chan--Paton pair \((\phi_1,\phi_2)\in\C^2\) is a chosen point of
  the formal polydisk, and
  \[
    J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}=f(\phi_1,\phi_2)
    \in\C
  \]
  is ordinary scalar evaluation modulo the constant.  \(J\) reduces
  to the closed-string evaluation map \(f\mapsto f(p)\) at a chosen
  polydisk point \(p\), and the Procesi--Razmyslov input is trivial.
  Every later non-commutative refinement of \(J\) collapses to this
  scalar evaluation when \(N=1\); the matrix structure enters with
  the brane stack.
\end{ex}

\begin{rmk}[Cyclic framing branch and the Hilbert scheme]\label{rmk:cyclic-framing-hilbert-branch}
  Adjoining a cyclic vector \(v\in\C^N\) with
  \(\C[\phi_1,\phi_2]\cdot v=\C^N\) cuts out the stable cyclic locus of
  the derived commuting variety stack
  \([\mu^{-1}_{\mathrm{der}}(0)/GL_N]\); on this locus the underived
  free \(GL_N\)-action gives the Hilbert scheme of points,
  \(\bigl[(\mu^{-1}(0)\times\C^N_{\mathrm{cyc}})/GL_N\bigr]
   =\Hilb^N(\C^2)\),
  in its Nakajima ADHM-quiver presentation.  The Moyal/Weyl deformation of the
  brane Poisson algebra restricts on this branch to the noncommutative
  Hilbert scheme of Nekrasov--Schwarz~\cite{nekrasov-schwarz-noncommutative},
  and the Capelli scalar \(\hbar N[\bar c]\) of
  Example~\ref{exa:linear-hamiltonians-anomaly} is its centre-of-mass
  \(U(1)\) charge.  The framing data is not part of the Dirac brane
  stack proper; it selects a stable open substack on which the derived
  fibre is underived.
\end{rmk}

\(J\) is exact at the level of the scalar trace algebra and fails at
the level of the full \(P_0\)-factorization centre: the chain-level
morphism from closed observables on \(L\) to the open factorization
centre fails for a structural reason.

\begin{rmk}[Cotangent obstruction beyond \(J\)]\label{rmk:formal-stalk-obstruction}\label{rmk:microscope-obstruction}
  Lemma~\ref{lem:probe-trace-microscope} resolves the scalar-trace
  generator question: scalar closed insertions \(F=J(f)\) commute with
  every open trace class up to the moment-map operation
  \(\{J(f),J(g)\}=J(\{f,g\})\) of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:witten-centrality-local-form} below.  The closed
  Hamiltonian BF sector on the polydisk and the open brane stack on
  \(L\) generate factorization algebras of classical observables,
  denoted \(\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{closed},H}\) and
  \(\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{open},N}\) (constructed in
  \S\ref{ssec:closed-mixed-hamiltonian},
  \S\ref{ssec:open-mixed-bft}); the \(P_0\)-factorization centre
  \(\mathrm Z^{P_0}_{\mathrm{fact}}\) of a factorization algebra is its
  centralizer for the shifted Poisson bracket and the factorization
  product (Problem~\ref{prob:formal-factorization-center}).  The
  strengthening sought beyond Lemma~\ref{lem:probe-trace-microscope}
  is a chain-level morphism
  \[
    \Phi_{\mathrm{cl}}\colon
    \mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{closed},H}\big|_L
    \longrightarrow
    \mathrm Z^{P_0}_{\mathrm{fact}}\!\bigl(\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{open},N}\bigr),
  \]
  asking for centrality homotopies linking closed insertions to
  \emph{arbitrary} open insertions, in particular to the principal-part
  cotangent currents \(\Theta_\rho\) (the boundary current attached to
  a residue \(\rho\in\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}\) by smearing,
  defined in Theorem~\ref{thm:reduced-principal-part-boundary-current}
  below).  Substitution into the Chan--Paton pair encodes the
  symbol-level information of \(f\), not the cotangent-direction
  antifield content of the closed BV algebra.  The obstruction is the unreduced
  cotangent centrality homotopy data of
  Problem~\ref{prob:formal-factorization-center} together with the
  Bochner--Martinelli obstruction vector
  \(\operatorname{Ob}^{\Pi}_{\mathrm{BM}}\), a six-component
  cohomology vector blocking strict all-window support-local current
  transfer to the brane line
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:bmk-finite-window-no-go}).  \(J\) is the scalar
  part of \(\Phi_{\mathrm{cl}}\); the cotangent part is the residual
  one, classified by the obstruction classes above.
\end{rmk}

\subsection{The shifted-cotangent BF Lie algebra}\label{ssec:hamiltonian-bf-algebra}

The holomorphic-symplectic Darboux normal form on
\((\widehat{\C^2}_0,\omega=dz_1\wedge dz_2)\) determines the closed BV
sector by three uniqueness statements. The Lie algebra of Hamiltonian
vector fields modulo constants is
\(\widehat{\mathfrak h}=\C[[z_1,z_2]]/\C\cdot 1\)
(Proposition~\ref{prop:hamiltonian-polyvector-reduction}). The
shifted-cotangent extension
\(\mathfrak g=\widehat{\mathfrak h}\ltimes
   \mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1]\) is the unique extension among
Lie extensions of \(\widehat{\mathfrak h}\) by an abelian odd ideal
carrying a non-degenerate equivariant pairing of cohomological
degree~\(-1\) (Theorem~\ref{thm:hbf-cotangent-uniqueness-summary}).
The Hamiltonian BF action is the unique compatible local classical
master equation (CME) action on this BV pair
(Theorem~\ref{thm:bf-action-uniqueness-qme}). The remainder of the
subsection records the three steps: the Lie algebra by Cartan's magic
formula and divergence reduction; the cotangent extension by the BV
canonical-transformation principle; the cotangent module by residue
duality.

\paragraph{The Lie algebra from Cartan's magic formula.}
An infinitesimal canonical transformation of
\((\widehat{\C^2}_0,\omega=dz_1\wedge dz_2)\) is a vector field
\(X\) annihilating \(\omega\) under the Lie derivative.  Cartan's
identity reduces this to closedness of \(\iota_X\omega\), which on the
formal polydisk is exactness: \(\iota_X\omega=-df\) for a unique
\(f\bmod\C\).  Hence the symmetry vector field is the Hamiltonian
vector field \(X_f\), and the kernel of \(f\mapsto X_f\) is the constant
line.  Equivalently, by
Proposition~\ref{prop:hamiltonian-polyvector-reduction}, the polyvector
divergence
\(\partial_\Omega\colon\mathbf{PV}^1\to\mathbf{PV}^0\) on the
polyvector-field complex \(\mathbf{PV}^\bullet\) of the polydisk
identifies its kernel with Hamiltonian vector fields modulo
constants.  The closed
Lie algebra is therefore
\begin{equation}\label{eq:hbar-Lie-algebra}
\bar A=\C[z_1,z_2]/\C\cdot1,\qquad
\widehat{\mathfrak h}=\C[[z_1,z_2]]/\C\cdot1,
\end{equation}
with the constant-Poisson bracket
\(\{f,g\}=\partial_{z_1}f\,\partial_{z_2}g
-\partial_{z_2}f\,\partial_{z_1}g\pmod\C\).  Constants do not vanish by
convention; they vanish because their Hamiltonian vector field is zero.
This is the kernel of the divergence-Hamiltonian identification.
Polynomial trace and Moyal calculations use the polynomial subalgebra
\(\bar A\); completed CE/Koszul constructions use the formal completion
\(\widehat{\mathfrak h}\).

\paragraph{The cotangent extension from canonical-transformation BV.}
A BV theory of canonical transformations promotes \(\widehat{\mathfrak h}\)
to a Lie algebra of fields by adjoining a degree-shifted cotangent
partner: each Hamiltonian ghost \(c\) acquires its antifield \(u\) in
the dual direction.  The Hamiltonian sector of BCOV/Kodaira--Spencer
gravity \cite{bcov} fixes this language.  The closed BV Lie algebra is
the shifted cotangent extension
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cotangent-Lie-algebra}
\mathfrak g=\widehat{\mathfrak h}\ltimes
  \mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1],
\end{equation}
with the Hamiltonian bracket on \(\widehat{\mathfrak h}\) and the
coadjoint action on \(\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1]\), and the
closed observables are the continuous Chevalley--Eilenberg cochains
\[
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{grav}}
  =C^\bullet_{\mathrm{CE},\mathrm{cont}}(\mathfrak g)
  =\widehat{\mathbf S}\bigl(\mathfrak g^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[-1]\bigr).
\]
The cochain CE/PV identity proved later
(Theorem~\ref{thm:reduced-ce-pv-central-operation}) lives on the
coordinate envelope
\(C^\bullet_{\mathrm{CE},\mathrm{coord}}(\mathfrak g)\)---the product
over finite graded-commutative words in the CE coordinates---and the
continuous CE cochains are its continuity-controlled subspace.

\paragraph{The cotangent module from residue duality.}
The shifted cotangent partner carries an antifield labelled by the
continuous dual \(\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}\).  This dual is not
another copy of Taylor polynomials.  The natural pairing
\(\widehat{\mathfrak h}\otimes\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}\to\C\)
must be continuous for the formal-disk filtration and equivariant for
the Hamiltonian action.  Both requirements name a unique module.

\begin{thm}[Defect momentum is the principal-part module]\label{thm:hbf-cotangent-uniqueness-summary}
  Let \(\mathfrak h=\C[[z_1,z_2]]/\C\cdot1\) carry the
  \(\mathfrak m\)-adic topology, \(\mathfrak m=(z_1,z_2)\).  The
  continuous \(\mathfrak h\)-equivariant dual is, up to the residue
  scalar of Theorem~\ref{thm:canonical-residue-pairing}, uniquely the
  Grothendieck--Matlis principal-part module
  \[
    \mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}\cong\mathcal P
    =\bigoplus_{a+b>0}\C\,\rho_{a,b},\qquad
    \rho_{a,b}=z_1^{-a-1}z_2^{-b-1}\,dz_1dz_2,
  \]
  with the residue pairing
  \(\langle\rho_{a,b},z_1^mz_2^n\rangle=\delta_{a,m}\delta_{b,n}\) and
  the residue-coadjoint action
  \(z_1^pz_2^q\cdot\rho_{a,b}=-(pb-qa+p-q)\rho_{a-p+1,b-q+1}\).
  Equivalently, no other continuous total-degree-zero
  \(\mathfrak h\)-module structure on a continuous dual of
  \(\widehat{\mathfrak h}\) is compatible with the residue pairing on
  the formal polydisk.
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
  This is Theorem~\ref{thm:principal-part-coadjoint-uniqueness}: local
  cohomology \cite{matlis-injective} of the maximal ideal
  \(\mathfrak m=(z_1,z_2)\) identifies the continuous dual of the
  formal completion with the principal-part module \(\mathcal P\); the
  residue pairing produces the displayed coadjoint formula on
  monomials, and any other continuous total-degree-zero
  \(\mathfrak h\)-equivariant pairing is a scalar multiple of the
  residue pairing.  After the residue scalar is fixed, the isomorphism
  is canonical.
\end{proof}

The PBW Rees algebra of \(\mathfrak g\) has special fibre
\begin{equation}
U_\hbar(\mathfrak g)/(\hbar)
  \cong\widehat{\mathbf S}\bigl(\widehat{\mathfrak h}
    \oplus\mathfrak h^\vee[1]\bigr),
\end{equation}
where \(U_\hbar(\mathfrak g)\) denotes the Rees deformation of the
universal enveloping algebra and \(\hbar\) the formal deformation
parameter.  The bidegree-\((a,b)\) generators of the one-\(\psi\)
sector inside this commutative special-fibre algebra are denoted
\(\Psi_{a,b}\); the deformation-level cotangent module is
\(P=\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}\), realised by the principal
parts \(\rho_{a,b}\) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:hbf-cotangent-uniqueness-summary}.
Theorem~\ref{thm:app-matlis-rees-fourier-bridge} supplies only the
Fourier-polarized Rees bridge between these label systems; no PBW
descendant is identified with a principal part as
\(\mathfrak h\)-modules.

A Taylor Hamiltonian is what the brane samples.  A principal part is
the dual distributional momentum concentrated at the brane: the
residue functional that detects a Taylor coefficient.  Cotangent
fields on a formal brane are residues, not polynomials.  Each
\(\rho_{a,b}\) is a defect-supported transverse mode: a distributional
object on the brane line whose holomorphic transverse behaviour is
encoded by a single principal-part coefficient.  An abstract copy
\(\bar A_{\mathrm{desc}}\) of \(\bar A\) sees the same labels through
the polynomial PBW special-fibre descendants \(\Psi_{k,l}\) just
introduced (full open-side construction:
Construction~\ref{constr:boundary-evaluation}); they carry the same
\((a,b)\)-bidegree as the principal parts but a different
\(\mathfrak h\)-module structure
(Corollary~\ref{cor:descendant-coadjoint-difference}); see
\S\ref{ssec:intro-notation-index} for the alphabet separation.

\begin{ex}[The lowest non-trivial residue mode]\label{ex:residue-mode-rho10}
  Take the linear Hamiltonian \(z_1\) and act on the lowest reduced
  residue \(\rho_{1,0}\).  The coadjoint formula of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:hbf-cotangent-uniqueness-summary} with
  \((p,q,a,b)=(1,0,1,0)\) gives
  \[
    z_1\cdot\rho_{1,0}=-(0-0+1-0)\rho_{1,1}=-\rho_{1,1}.
  \]
  The polynomial PBW class \(\Psi_{1,0}\), labelled identically,
  carries a different action: under the open one-\(\psi\) descendant
  module of Proposition~\ref{prop:open-descendant-action} the linear
  Hamiltonian shifts \(\Psi_{k,l}\) by the bracket on the
  open-trace ring, not by the residue.  Same label, two
  \(\mathfrak h\)-module structures.  The residue mode is what the
  defect momentum sees; the polynomial PBW class is what the open
  trace ring records.  The Fourier-polarized Rees bridge of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:app-matlis-rees-fourier-bridge} is the
  intermodule passage between the two.  This worked case computes
  the residue-side action of \(\rho_{a,b}\) explicitly, complementing
  Remark~\ref{rmk:intro-symbol-separation}.
\end{ex}

\begin{rmk}[Scalar Hamiltonian and centre-of-mass term]\label{rmk:scalar-hamiltonian-center-of-mass}
  In \(\bar A\), \(\{z_1,z_2\}=1\equiv0\) by quotient.  On the
  unreduced brane phase space,
  \(\{\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_1),\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_2)\}=N\).  Constants
  vanish closed-side; the same constant survives open-side as the
  brane centre-of-mass symplectic pair.  Every bracket involving
  linear Hamiltonians is read after projection to the Hamiltonian
  quotient by scalar trace classes.  Quantisation does not soften this
  asymmetry; it returns it as the Capelli shift
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:quantum-classical-anomaly} below; cf.\
  Remark~\ref{rmk:normal-ordering-obstruction}).
\end{rmk}

\begin{rmk}[Cotangent obstruction]\label{rmk:hbf-cotangent-obstruction}
  Theorem~\ref{thm:hbf-cotangent-uniqueness-summary} resolves the
  identification of \(\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}\) but not the
  unreduced lift of the cotangent factorisation centre.  Promoting the
  closed cochain CE/PV identity
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:reduced-ce-pv-central-operation}) to a chain-level
  morphism
  \(\Phi_{\mathrm{cl}}\colon
   \mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{closed},H}|_L
   \to\mathrm Z^{P_0}_{\mathrm{fact}}(\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\mathrm{open},N})\)
  asks for centrality homotopies on the cotangent (one-antifield) sector.
  The exact obstruction datum is named in
  Problem~\ref{prob:formal-factorization-center}; the strict
  all-window Bochner--Martinelli current transfer obstruction is
  \(\operatorname{Ob}^{\Pi}_{\mathrm{BM}}\).  A global central
  extension to the bracket-admissible
  \(B\subset\mathrm{PV}_{\mathrm{coord}}(A_{\partial,\mathrm{coord}})\)
  of Definition~\ref{defn:bracket-kernel-admissible} requires the
  vanishing of \(\operatorname{Ob}^{\Pi}_{\mathrm{BM}}\) on the strict
  ambient product completion.
\end{rmk}

\subsection{Notation for the local theorem}\label{ssec:intro-notation-index}

The local model uses three disjoint alphabets.  Letters from one
alphabet are operations; letters from another are measurements; the
remaining letters are distributional cotangent modes.  Coincident
\((a,b)\)-labels across alphabets are an indexing convention, never an
identification.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.28\textwidth}p{0.62\textwidth}@{}}
\hline
\textbf{Symbol} & \textbf{Meaning}\\
\hline
\(R=\C[[z_1,z_2]]\) &
Complete local ring of the formal symplectic polydisk.\\
\(\mathfrak h=R/\C\cdot1\) &
Reduced Hamiltonian Lie algebra.\\
\(\bar A=\C[z_1,z_2]/\C\cdot1\) &
Polynomial Hamiltonian subalgebra used in trace and Moyal computations.\\
\(\mathfrak g=\mathfrak h\ltimes\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1]\) &
Shifted cotangent Lie algebra of Hamiltonian canonical transformations.\\
\(c^I\) (operation) &
CE coordinate dual to a Hamiltonian generator
\(H_I\in\mathfrak h\); produces a Schouten vector field.\\
\(u_I\) (measurement) &
CE coordinate dual to a shifted-cotangent generator
\(\eta_I\in\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1]\); produces a boundary
Hamiltonian observable.\\
\(\theta^I,\,O_I\) &
PV coordinates; the CE/PV map sends \(c^I\mapsto\theta^I\),
\(u_I\mapsto O_I\).\\
\(O_{a,b}\) (measurement) &
Open trace observable
\(\overline{\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_1^a\phi_2^b)}\), \(a+b>0\).\\
\(\Psi_{a,b}\) (PBW class) &
Primitive one-\(\psi\) PBW special-fibre descendant in bidegree
\((a,b)\).\\
\(\rho_{a,b}\) (cotangent mode) &
Principal part \(z_1^{-a-1}z_2^{-b-1}dz_1dz_2\), basis element of
\(\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}\).\\
\(B_f(a)\) &
Smeared boundary Hamiltonian
\(\int a(t)\overline{\operatorname{Tr}f(\phi_1(t),\phi_2(t))}\,dt\).\\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\begin{rmk}[Two avatars of a Hamiltonian: operation and measurement]\label{rmk:intro-symbol-separation}
  \emph{(i) The two CE coordinates of a Hamiltonian.}  The boundary
  Hamiltonian observable on the brane is the image of the
  shifted-cotangent coordinate \(u_f\); the Hamiltonian CE coordinate
  \(c_f\) acts on observables as the Schouten vector field
  \(\theta^I\).  Their bracket compatibility
  \([\theta^I,O_J]_S=\delta^I_J\) is the Schouten--observable pairing
  organising the \(P_0\)-centrality homotopies of
  Problem~\ref{prob:formal-factorization-center}; the dictionary is
  Lemma~\ref{lem:type-discipline}.

  \emph{(ii) Bidegree coincidence \(\Psi_{a,b}\leftrightarrow\rho_{a,b}\).}
  The polynomial PBW descendant \(\Psi_{a,b}\) and the principal part
  \(\rho_{a,b}\) share the bidegree \((a,b)\) and carry distinct
  \(\mathfrak h\)-module structures
  (Corollary~\ref{cor:descendant-coadjoint-difference}), connected by
  the Fourier-polarized Rees bridge of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:app-matlis-rees-fourier-bridge}.  See
  Example~\ref{ex:residue-mode-rho10} for the lowest non-trivial
  worked case.
\end{rmk}

\subsection{The reduced CE/PV measurement}\label{ssec:reduced-ce-pv-measurement}

The closed BV theory has two coordinate types, and the brane reads only
one of them.  Hamiltonian ghosts \(c^I\)---dual to generators
\(H_I\in\widehat{\mathfrak h}\)---generate the closed motion on the
polydisk; cotangent coordinates \(u_I\)---dual to the shifted antifield
generators in \(\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1]\)---are the
measurable boundary observables.  Lemma~\ref{lem:probe-trace-microscope}
gives the trace substitution
\[
  J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}
\]
on the second alphabet.  Centrality is not a separate condition: it
is the moment-map identity
\(\{J(f),J(g)\}_\partial=J(\{f,g\}_{\mathfrak h})\), read locally on
the brane.  Closed Hamiltonians act through \(c_f\); brane
measurements record \(u_f\); below, the symbols
\(c_f,u_f,\theta_f,O_f\) refer back to Lemma~\ref{lem:type-discipline}.

The CE/PV comparison is graded into five levels at separate
admissibility hypotheses.
\[
  \begin{array}{c|l|l}
    \text{level} & \text{statement} & \text{admissibility} \\[2pt]
    \hline\\[-9pt]
    1 & \text{coordinate cochain identity }
        \Phi_{\mathrm{coord}}(c^I)=\theta^I,\,
        \Phi_{\mathrm{coord}}(u_I)=O_I
      & \text{none (unconditional)} \\
    2 & P_0\text{-bracket enhancement on the chosen sub-PV model}
      & \text{bracket-admissibility of } B \\
    3 & \text{Maurer--Cartan kernel compatibility}
      & \text{kernel datum }(B,\mathcal K_B) \\
    4 & \text{bar-cobar / enveloping upgrade }
        \Omega C^\bullet_{\mathrm{CE}}\xrightarrow{\sim}A
      & \text{Tate / nilpotent / weighted datum} \\
    5 & \text{open-trace }A_\infty\text{-Koszul upgrade}
      & A_\infty\text{-acceptance datum }
        (A_{\mathrm{op}},C_{\mathrm{op}},B_{\mathrm{CE/PV}},\tau_{\mathrm{op}})
  \end{array}
\]
The unconditional content is level 1.  Levels~2--5 are conditional,
each on a separate admissibility datum stated in
Theorem~\ref{thm:universal-ce-pv-koszul-criterion}: \(B\) is a graded
sub-Lie algebra of the polyvector coordinate ring
\(\mathrm{PV}_{\mathrm{coord}}\) closed under the Schouten bracket
(bracket-admissibility,
Definition~\ref{defn:bracket-kernel-admissible}); \(\mathcal K_B\) is a
target completion of the diagonal tensor product in which the Casimir
converges (kernel-admissibility); the Tate / nilpotent / weighted datum
specifies a bar-cobar replacement of the closed CE complex with a
conilpotent endpoint and \(\varprojlim^1\)-vanishing; and the
\(A_\infty\)-acceptance datum
\((A_{\mathrm{op}},C_{\mathrm{op}},B_{\mathrm{CE/PV}},\tau_{\mathrm{op}})\)
collects an open-trace \(A_\infty\) algebra
\(A_{\mathrm{op}}\), a Koszul-dual coalgebra \(C_{\mathrm{op}}\), the
matching CE/PV bracket model \(B_{\mathrm{CE/PV}}\), and a twisting
cochain \(\tau_{\mathrm{op}}\).  References below to ``the CE/PV
comparison'' identify their level explicitly.

\begin{lem}[Two CE coordinates of a Hamiltonian]\label{lem:type-discipline}
  A Hamiltonian \(f\in\mathfrak h\) carries two CE coordinates on the
  closed shifted-cotangent BV side.
  \begin{itemize}
    \item As generator of canonical transformations:
      \(c_f\in\mathfrak h^\vee[-1]\) is the Hamiltonian-dual CE
      coordinate, and
      \(\Phi_{\mathrm{coord}}(c_f)=\theta_f\), the Schouten vector
      field on the boundary observable algebra.
    \item As closed cotangent insertion:
      \(u_f\in\mathfrak h\subset\mathfrak g[1]\) is the
      shifted-cotangent-dual coordinate, and
      \(\Phi_{\mathrm{coord}}(u_f)=O_f\), the boundary observable
      \(J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}\) read on
      the brane.
  \end{itemize}
  Closed Hamiltonians act through \(c_f\); brane measurements record
  \(u_f\); the CE/PV theorem
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:universal-ce-pv-koszul-criterion},
  Theorem~\ref{thm:reduced-ce-pv-central-operation}) implements the
  duality.
\end{lem}

\begin{proof}
  Theorem~\ref{thm:coordinate-free-cotangent-ce-pv} fixes the generator
  signs in the finite-dimensional case:
  \(\Phi_{\mathfrak l}(c^\xi)=\theta^\xi\),
  \(\Phi_{\mathfrak l}(u_x)=O_x\).
  Theorem~\ref{thm:coordinate-free-closed-open-synthesis} extends this
  generator assignment to the formal-polydisk coordinate envelope.
  Equating \(c_f\) with \(O_f\) breaks the coordinate
  bracket-compatibility
  \([\theta^I,O_J]_S=\delta^I_J\), which is what every
  \(P_0\)-centrality homotopy uses
  (clause~(2) of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:universal-ce-pv-koszul-criterion}).  No central
  extension or kernel datum can repair the collision: the failure is
  already at the bracket on generators.
\end{proof}

\begin{rmk}[Generator table]\label{rmk:type-discipline-table}
  \[
    \begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
      \text{symbol} & \text{CE coordinate on}
        & \Phi_{\mathrm{coord}}\text{-image}
        & \text{physical role}\\[2pt]
      \hline\\[-9pt]
      c_f & \mathfrak h^\vee[-1]
        & \theta_f\in\mathrm{Vect}(\widehat{\mathbf S}(\bar A))
        & \text{generator of canonical motion}\\
      u_f & (\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1])^\vee
        & O_f=J(f)\in\widehat{\mathbf S}(\bar A)
        & \text{brane-line measurement}
    \end{array}
  \]
\end{rmk}

\begin{lem}[Bracket on observables]\label{lem:bracket-on-observables}
  For \(f,g\in\mathfrak h\),
  \[
    \theta_f(O_g)=O_{\{f,g\}}.
  \]
  Equivalently, the Schouten field \(\theta_f=\Phi_{\mathrm{coord}}(c_f)\)
  acts on the boundary observable
  \(O_g=\Phi_{\mathrm{coord}}(u_g)=J(g)\) as the Hamiltonian
  derivation \(g\mapsto\{f,g\}\).
\end{lem}

\begin{proof}
  On generators \(c_f,u_g\) of \(C^\bullet_{\mathrm{CE},\mathrm{coord}}(\mathfrak g)\)
  the CE differential gives \(d_{\mathrm{CE}}u_g(c_f)=u_{\{f,g\}}\).
  The CE/PV identification
  Theorem~\ref{thm:coordinate-free-cotangent-ce-pv} sends
  \(c_f\mapsto\theta_f\) and \(u_g\mapsto O_g\), and the Schouten
  pairing \([\theta_f,O_g]_S=\theta_f(O_g)\) on
  \(\mathrm{PV}(\widehat{\mathbf S}(\bar A))\) carries the same
  derivation on linear functions; the equality on the brane side is
  the moment-map identity
  \(\{J(f),J(g)\}_\partial=J(\{f,g\}_{\mathfrak h})\) of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:bulk-boundary}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The CE/PV dictionary.}
On generators the cochain CE/PV identification of
Theorem~\ref{thm:reduced-ce-pv-central-operation} is
\[
  c^I\longmapsto\theta^I,\qquad u_I\longmapsto O_I.
\]
This is Lemma~\ref{lem:type-discipline} written on a basis: the
Hamiltonian-dual CE coordinate produces a Schouten vector field, the
shifted-cotangent-dual CE coordinate produces a boundary Hamiltonian
observable.  The brane substitution is the image of \(u_f\), not of
\(c_f\).

\paragraph{Word ordering is BRST-exact, the bracket records the symbol.}
At finite \(N\), \(J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}\)
requires a word ordering of the noncommuting matrices in
\(f(\phi_1,\phi_2)\).  The choice is irrelevant in
\(H^0(\mathcal R_N^{GL_N},Q)\): adjacent swaps are exact via
\(Q\,\operatorname{Tr}(\psi vu)=\operatorname{Tr}([\phi_1,\phi_2]vu)\)
of \eqref{eq:open-Q}, so \(J(f)\) depends only on the symbol \(f\).
The matrix Poisson bracket induced by
\(\{(\phi_1)^i{}_j,(\phi_2)^k{}_l\}=\delta^i_l\delta^k_j\) is
\[
  \{F,G\}=\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(
    \partial_{\phi_1}F\cdot\partial_{\phi_2}G
    -\partial_{\phi_2}F\cdot\partial_{\phi_1}G\bigr)
\]
with cyclic matrix derivatives.  On monomials,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bracket-coefficient}
\bigl\{\overline{\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_1^k\phi_2^l)},
       \overline{\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_1^m\phi_2^n)}\bigr\}
  =\overline{(kn-lm)\operatorname{Tr}
    (\phi_1^{k+m-1}\phi_2^{l+n-1})},
\end{equation}
and assembling over the symbol algebra after scalar reduction,
\begin{equation}
\bigl\{J(f),J(g)\bigr\}_\partial
  =J\bigl(\{f,g\}_{\mathfrak h}\bigr).
\end{equation}
\(J\) is a Lie algebra morphism
\(\bar A\to H^0(\mathcal R_N^{GL_N},Q)\) modulo the central direction
\S\ref{subsec:scalar-anomaly} diagnoses.

\paragraph{Why finite Taylor truncation fails.}
The naive finite-rank reduction of \(\widehat{\mathfrak h}\) is its
Taylor-degree truncation \(\widehat{\mathfrak h}/\mathfrak m^{N+1}\).  This is not a
Lie quotient: linear Hamiltonians \(z_1,z_2\) generate brackets of
strictly lower Taylor degree, the projected brackets fail Jacobi after
truncation, and the diagonal cotangent Casimir
\(C_{\widehat{\mathfrak h}}=\sum_{d\ge1}e_{d,i}\otimes e^i_d\) misses
the strict product/direct-sum endpoint.  Finite coordinate projections
exist only as coefficient tests.  They assemble to the completed
coordinate CE/PV isomorphism
\[
  \Phi_{\mathrm{coord}}\colon
  C^\bullet_{\mathrm{CE},\mathrm{coord}}(\mathfrak g)
  \xrightarrow{\sim}
  \mathrm{PV}_{\mathrm{coord}}(A_{\partial,\mathrm{coord}})
\]
of Theorem~\ref{thm:reduced-ce-pv-central-operation}.  A \(P_0\)
comparison is asserted only after a bracket-admissible
\(B\subset\mathrm{PV}_{\mathrm{coord}}(A_{\partial,\mathrm{coord}})\)
is chosen (Definition~\ref{defn:bracket-kernel-admissible}); a
Maurer--Cartan kernel is asserted only after the diagonal tensor
converges in a kernel-admissible tensor product.  Both are part of the
reduced Hamiltonian central-operation model and not the strict ambient
product.

\begin{thm}[Witten centrality on the Dirac brane: the local moment-map measurement]
  \label{thm:witten-centrality-local-form}
  In the reduced classical \(P_0\) factorization algebra
  \(A_\partial\) on the brane line, the substitution map
  \(J\colon\bar A\to A_\partial\),
  \(f\mapsto B_f=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}\),
  realises every closed Hamiltonian \(f\in\bar A\) as a boundary
  observable whose ordered products and \(P_0\)-bracket with smeared
  Hamiltonian insertions \(B_g(b)\) are the moment-map operations
  \begin{equation}
    \{B_f(a),B_g(b)\}=B_{\{f,g\}}(ab),\qquad
    F\cdot G\simeq(-1)^{|F||G|}G\cdot F\ \text{(scalar \(F\))}.
  \end{equation}
  In particular \(J\) lands inside the reduced \(P_0\)-central-operation
  complex of \(A_\partial\), \(J(f)\) is null-homotopic for every
  scalar (constant) \(f\), and \(J\) is Witten's classical centrality
  computation \cite{witten-cs} read in the local algebraic model
  \(Z^{P_0}_{\mathrm{red,Ham}}(A_{\partial,\mathrm{coord}};B)=B\).
  The unreduced \(P_0\)-factorization centre lift, including centrality
  homotopies against arbitrary open observables and principal-part
  cotangent currents, is exactly
  Problem~\ref{prob:formal-factorization-center}.
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
  The brane equal-time Poisson bracket induced by the symplectic
  potential \(\theta_N=\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_1\,d\phi_2)\) of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:dirac-probe-reduction} is, on two brane-time points
  \((t,t')\),
  \[
    \{(\phi_1)^i{}_j(t),(\phi_2)^k{}_l(t')\}
      =\delta^i_l\delta^k_j\,\delta(t-t').
  \]
  Smearing by \(a(t)b(t')\) replaces \(\delta(t-t')\) by the product
  \(ab\); contraction of the matrix derivatives of
  \(\operatorname{Tr}f(\phi_1,\phi_2)\) and
  \(\operatorname{Tr}g(\phi_1,\phi_2)\) returns the constant Poisson
  bracket on the symbols, evaluated again on the probe pair.  This
  produces the moment-map identity
  \(\{B_f(a),B_g(b)\}=B_{\{f,g\}}(ab)\); on monomials the explicit
  coefficient \(kn-lm\) is the open trace bracket
  \eqref{eq:bracket-coefficient}.  Cyclic invariance and the
  \(Q\)-exact swaps reduce every monomial bracket to its symbol-level
  Hamiltonian bracket on \(\bar A\).

  Constants in \(\bar A\) have zero Hamiltonian vector field, so the
  scalar bracket component is null-homotopic; the central direction
  \(\C\cdot J(1)\) is exactly the U(1) centre treated in
  \S\ref{subsec:scalar-anomaly}.  The locally constant brane-line
  factorisation product compares two ordered products of a closed and
  an open insertion; the homotopy connecting them is the Hochschild
  centrality datum of the associated \(E_1\)-algebra stalk, and the
  \(P_0\) refinement imposes the same compatibility for the matrix
  Poisson bracket.  Both are produced by the moment-map identity above.

  The unreduced cotangent statement requires centrality homotopies for
  arbitrary open observables, including the principal-part cotangent
  currents \(\Theta_\rho\) of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:reduced-principal-part-boundary-current}.  The
  trace substitution \(J\) provides the scalar bracket sector; it
  does not produce these cotangent homotopies, the obstruction of
  Problem~\ref{prob:formal-factorization-center}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The U(1) centre-of-mass cocycle}\label{subsec:scalar-anomaly}

A constant Hamiltonian \(c\in\C\subset\C[z_1,z_2]\) acts trivially on the
polydisk: its Hamiltonian vector field vanishes, so it sits in the kernel of
the canonical-transformation reduction
\eqref{eq:hbar-Lie-algebra}.  The same constant pulled back along the
substitution \(z_i\mapsto\phi_i\) becomes \(c\cdot I\), and the matrix
Poisson partner of the symplectic potential is
\[
  \{\operatorname{Tr}\phi_1,\operatorname{Tr}\phi_2\}
    =\operatorname{Tr}(I\cdot I)=N,
\]
the brane centre-of-mass charge.  Closed-side: invisible.  Open-side:
visible, equal to \(N\).  The asymmetry is a single Lie cohomology
class \([\bar c]\in H^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\bar A,\C)\), realised both as
an abelian-extension obstruction on \(\bar A\) and as the U(1) trace
centre of \(\mathfrak{gl}_N\), and it survives Moyal quantisation as
the order-\(\hbar\) Capelli shift; the same cocycle is detected
algebraically, Lie-theoretically, in the trace algebra, and in the
quantum Weyl commutator
\([\Phi_\hbar(z_1),\Phi_\hbar(z_2)]_\star=\hbar N\cdot I\) in $M_N(\C)$ --- four equivalent
sightings of one class
(Theorem~\ref{thm:app-scalar-anomaly-transgression}).

\paragraph{The closed--open mismatch cocycle.}
Write \(\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}=\C[z_1,z_2]\) for the unreduced
polynomial Hamiltonian Lie algebra, constants kept; the bracket
\(\{f,g\}=\partial_{z_1}f\,\partial_{z_2}g
-\partial_{z_2}f\,\partial_{z_1}g\) again takes values in
\(\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}\).  Define
\[
  \omega\colon
  \mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}\otimes\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}
    \longrightarrow\C,\qquad
   \omega(f,g)=[\,\{f,g\}\,]_0,
\]
the constant Taylor coefficient of the bracket.  On monomials,
\[
  \omega(z_1^kz_2^l,z_1^mz_2^n)=
  \begin{cases}
    kn-lm, & (k+m,l+n)=(1,1),\\
    0, & \text{otherwise},
  \end{cases}
\]
so \(\omega\) is concentrated in the unique bidegree where the bracket
multidegree \((k+m-1,l+n-1)\) lands at \((0,0)\): the constant-Taylor
direction the canonical-transformation reduction
\eqref{eq:hbar-Lie-algebra} discards.  This is the direction on which
the closed and open sides disagree.

\begin{ex}[Linear Hamiltonians and the brane centre of mass]\label{ex:linear-hamiltonian-com}
  The two linear Hamiltonians \(f_1=z_1\) and \(f_2=z_2\) measure
  \[
    J(z_1)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}\phi_1},
    \qquad
    J(z_2)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}\phi_2},
  \]
  the brane centre of mass in the two transverse directions.  On the
  unreduced phase space the matrix Poisson bracket of these two
  observables is
  \[
    \{\operatorname{Tr}\phi_1,\operatorname{Tr}\phi_2\}
      =\operatorname{Tr}(I\cdot I)=N,
  \]
  so the brane centre-of-mass pair is a Heisenberg pair with charge
  \(N\): the open-side image of the unique cocycle bidegree
  \(\omega(z_1,z_2)=1\).  Closed-side, \(\{z_1,z_2\}=1\equiv0\) in
  \(\bar A\) by quotient.  Moyal quantisation returns the same scalar
  at order \(\hbar\): on the matrix-brane realization
  $\Phi_\hbar(z_i)=\phi_i$ in $M_N(\C)$ the commutator
  \[
    [\Phi_\hbar(z_1),\Phi_\hbar(z_2)]_\star=\hbar N\cdot I_N
  \]
  truncates exactly at first order because the Moyal expansion needs
  at least one second derivative on each tensor factor and the linear
  Hamiltonian has none (Theorem~\ref{thm:quantum-classical-anomaly}).
  The same anomalous scalar is detected four times: on the closed Lie
  cohomology side, on the open trace algebra side, on the Moyal/Weyl
  side, and on the modular projective-curvature side.  These are the
  four equivalent sightings of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:capelli-omega-central-identification}.
\end{ex}

\begin{lem}[Closed--open mismatch lemma]\label{lem:omega-cocycle}
  The form $\omega$ is a Lie 2-cocycle on $\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}$:
  exact on the unreduced algebra, not exact after the constant-quotient.
  Equivalently:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item on $\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}$, $\omega=\delta\eta$ with
      $\eta(f)=-[f]_0$;
    \item on $\bar A=\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}/\C\cdot 1$, the
      restricted cocycle represents a nonzero class
      $[\bar c]\in H^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\bar A,\C)$.
  \end{itemize}
  The class $[\bar c]$ is the closed-side obstruction blocking the
  descent of $\eta$ past the constant line.  Up to the multiplier
  $\operatorname{Tr}I=N$ it is the open-side scalar
  $\{\operatorname{Tr}\phi_1,\operatorname{Tr}\phi_2\}=N$ pulled back
  through the boundary evaluation map of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:u1-center-anomaly-open}: the same scalar mismatch,
  read on each side.
\end{lem}

\begin{proof}
  \emph{Cocycle identity.}  Project each term of the Jacobi identity
  on $\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}$ to its constant Taylor coefficient:
  $$\omega(f,\{g,h\})+\omega(g,\{h,f\})+\omega(h,\{f,g\})=0.$$
  Antisymmetry of $\omega$ is inherited from antisymmetry of the
  bracket.  Hence
  $\omega\in Z^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}},\C)$.

  \emph{Exactness on the unreduced algebra.}  Set $\eta(f)=-[f]_0$.
  Then for all $f,g\in\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}$,
  $\delta\eta(f,g)=-\eta(\{f,g\})=[\{f,g\}]_0=\omega(f,g)$.

  \emph{Non-exactness after constant-quotient.}  The cocycle $\omega$
  descends to $\bar A$ because it already vanishes on the constant
  line.  The primitive $\eta$ does not, because $\eta(1)=-1$.  Suppose
  some $\bar\eta\colon\bar A\to\C$ primitive of $[\bar c]$ existed;
  lift to $\tilde\eta\colon\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}\to\C$ by setting
  $\tilde\eta(1)=0$.  Then
  $\tilde\eta(\{z_1,z_2\})=\tilde\eta(1)=0$, while
  $\omega(z_1,z_2)=1$, contradicting $\delta\tilde\eta=\omega$.

  \emph{Open-side image.}  The closed cocycle on the unit bidegree is
  $\omega(z_1,z_2)=1$; the boundary evaluation
  $\partial_{\mathrm{bb},N}^{\mathrm{full}}$ of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:u1-center-anomaly-open} multiplies by
  $\operatorname{Tr}I=N$ to give
  $\{\operatorname{Tr}\phi_1,\operatorname{Tr}\phi_2\}
  =\operatorname{Tr}(I\cdot I)=N$.  Same axis, two readings.
\end{proof}

The class \([\bar c]\) reads as Chevalley--Eilenberg cohomology on the
closed side and as the \(\mathfrak{gl}_N\) trace centre on the open
side.

\begin{thm}[Closed-side face: the scalar abelian extension]\label{thm:u1-center-anomaly}
  The class $[\bar c]\in H^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\bar A,\C)$ of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:omega-cocycle} is the obstruction class to splitting
  the abelian Lie extension
  $$0\longrightarrow \C\cdot 1\longrightarrow \mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}
    \longrightarrow \bar A\longrightarrow 0.$$
  Equivalently, $\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}\cong\bar A_{[\bar c]}
  =\bar A\oplus\C K$ as Lie algebras, with bracket
  $$[\,(f,aK),(g,bK)\,]=(\{f,g\}_{\bar A},\,\bar c(f,g)\,K),$$
  and $\C\cdot[\bar c]\subset H^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\bar A,\C)$ is the
  distinguished anomaly line attached to the constant-Hamiltonian
  central extension.
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
  The constant Hamiltonian $1\in\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}$ has zero
  Hamiltonian vector field, so $\C\cdot 1$ is central; the displayed
  sequence is a central extension and its class is its standard
  Chevalley--Eilenberg obstruction.
  Computing on monomial generators,
  $$[z_1^kz_2^l,z_1^mz_2^n]_{\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}}
    =(kn-lm)z_1^{k+m-1}z_2^{l+n-1},$$
  and projecting to the central line $\C\cdot 1$ keeps the
  $(0,0)$-Taylor coefficient.  This is the cocycle $\omega$, hence the
  extension class is $[\bar c]$.  Non-triviality is
  Lemma~\ref{lem:omega-cocycle}; the scalar-extension line
  $\C\cdot[\bar c]\subset H^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\bar A,\C)$ is therefore
  nonzero, and splitting the corresponding single extension is the
  problem of lifting back to $\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{poly}}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}[Open-side face: the U(1) trace centre at finite $N$]\label{thm:u1-center-anomaly-open}
  At finite $N$ the matrix Poisson bracket
  $\{F,G\}=\mathrm{Tr}(\partial_{\phi_1}F\,\partial_{\phi_2}G
  -\partial_{\phi_2}F\,\partial_{\phi_1}G)$ on
  $\C[\mathfrak{gl}_N\oplus\mathfrak{gl}_N]^{GL_N}$ satisfies
  $$\{\mathrm{Tr}\,\phi_1,\mathrm{Tr}\,\phi_2\}=N$$
  in the unreduced ring; this scalar vanishes in the reduced quotient
  $H^0_{\mathrm{red}}=H^0/\C\cdot\mathrm{Tr}(1)$.  Under the boundary
  evaluation map
  $$\partial_{\mathrm{bb},N}^{\mathrm{full}}\colon\C[z_1,z_2]\longrightarrow
    \C[\mathfrak{gl}_N^2]^{GL_N},\qquad
    f\longmapsto \mathrm{Tr}\,f(\phi_1,\phi_2),$$
  the closed cocycle $\omega$ on $\bar A$ multiplies by
  $\operatorname{Tr}I=N$ to give the open trace bracket on the central
  direction; the resulting central extension of the open trace algebra
  is therefore the image of $[\bar c]$.  Since
  $\C\cdot I\subset\mathfrak{gl}_N$ is the U(1) centre with character
  $\operatorname{Tr}$, the brane realisation of $[\bar c]$ is exactly
  the U(1) centre of $\mathfrak{gl}_N$ with charge $N$.
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
  On the linear generators
  $\partial_{\phi_1}\mathrm{Tr}\,\phi_1=I$ and
  $\partial_{\phi_2}\mathrm{Tr}\,\phi_2=I$; the cross derivatives
  vanish.  The matrix Poisson bracket is therefore
  $\mathrm{Tr}(I\cdot I)=N$ in the unreduced ring and zero modulo
  $\C\cdot\mathrm{Tr}(1)$.  The boundary evaluation map sends
