  the column of \(D^2_{02,20}\) and the residual are
  \[
    A_D^2=(-2,2,1)^T,\qquad r_2=(2,-2,0)^T .
  \]
  The equation \(A_D^2c=-r_2\) has no solution in this free
  presentation.  The cokernel
  \[
    \widetilde\lambda_{02,\mathfrak b}
    =
    \frac12(E^2_{\nu_{02}})^*
    +(\mathfrak b^2_{02,20})^*
  \]
  satisfies
  \[
    \widetilde\lambda_{02,\mathfrak b}A_D^2=0,\qquad
    \widetilde\lambda_{02,\mathfrak b}(r_2)=1.
  \]
  Thus the lower source primitive is a genuine local-functional primitive
  only in the quotient where \(\mathfrak b^2_{02,20}=0\), or after a
  scalar-zero local counterterm \(B^2_{02,20}\) with
  \(d_{\mathrm{ns},2}B^2_{02,20}=-\mathfrak b^2_{02,20}\) is supplied,
  with locality and transport compatibility.

  In the current Bianchi-exposed finite-row completion no such
  \(B^2_{02,20}\) exists.  The only degree-zero lower column is
  \(D^2_{02,20}\), so the desired Bianchi-counterterm target is the
  vector
  \[
    -\mathfrak b^2_{02,20}=(0,0,-1)^T .
  \]
  It is not in the image of \(A_D^2\), since
  \[
    \widetilde\lambda_{02,\mathfrak b}A_D^2=0,\qquad
    \widetilde\lambda_{02,\mathfrak b}((0,0,-1)^T)=-1 .
  \]
  The minimal controlled enlargement must therefore add a genuine
  scalar-zero local-functional column
  \[
    A_B^2=(0,0,-1)^T,\qquad
    d_{\mathrm{ns},2}B^2_{02,20}
      =-\mathfrak b^2_{02,20},
  \]
  with regular-density or wavefront-admissible locality and compatible
  finite-window transport.  In a tower this requires
  \[
    \Delta^1_{M,N}
    :=
    -\pi_{M,N}\mathfrak b^M+\mathfrak b^N
    =
    d_{\mathrm{ns},N}(\pi_{M,N}B^M-B^N)
  \]
  together with vanishing of the \(H^1\) Bianchi transport class on the
  right-hand side and then of the resulting secondary
  \(\varprojlim^1H^0\)-class after any supplied transport correction.
\end{prop}

\begin{proof}
  The one-row complex used here has no degree-zero term and has
  one degree-one basis vector.  Hence
  \(H^1(\mathcal Q^\bullet_{\theta_3,M})\cong
  \C\mathsf E_{\theta_3,M}\).  The covector
  \[
    \ell_{\theta_3}(\mathsf E_{\theta_3,M})=1
  \]
  satisfies \(\ell_{\theta_3}d_M=0\) and evaluates nontrivially on the
  residual, so it is a left-cokernel functional for the nonzero
  fixed-window \(H^1\)-class.

  After adjoining any finite collection of degree-zero candidate rows,
  the fixed-window question is the elementary linear system
  \[
    A^Mc^M=-r^M,
  \]
  where \(A^M\) is the matrix of \(d_M\) from candidate rows to residual
  rows and \(r^M\) is the coordinate vector of
  \(\mathfrak o^{\mathrm{ns}}_{\theta_3,M}\).  A CE ancestor or local
  counterterm with differential coefficient \(-1\) is exactly a column
  of \(A^M\) equal to \(-\mathsf E_{\theta_3,M}\), so \(c^M=1\) solves
  the equation.  If the candidate is named but this differential entry is
  absent, the old covector \(\ell_{\theta_3}\) still annihilates the
  image of \(A^M\) and still evaluates to \(1\) on \(r^M\); the
  obstruction survives.

  The lower source-coordinate candidate illustrates the distinction
  between source exactness and local-functional exactness.  The source
  calculation gives
  \(d_{\mathrm{CE}}\zeta^0_2=2\zeta_{\nu_{02}}-2\zeta_{\nu_{20}}\), but
  \(K^2_{\Theta_3}\) is not a chain map until its Hom-valued Bianchi
  defect is killed.  In the Bianchi-exposed basis the matrix equation is
  \(A_D^2c=-r_2\), with \(A_D^2=(-2,2,1)^T\) and \(r_2=(2,-2,0)^T\).
  The first two coordinates force \(c=1\), while the Bianchi coordinate
  forces \(c=0\).  Equivalently
  \(\widetilde\lambda_{02,\mathfrak b}A_D^2=0\) and
  \(\widetilde\lambda_{02,\mathfrak b}(r_2)=1\).  Hence this computation
  gives an exact obstruction class, not a completed
  local-functional counterterm.

  The same cokernel proves the no-\(B^2_{02,20}\) statement in the
  current completion.  The target vector \((0,0,-1)^T\) would require a
  scalar \(c\) with \(c(-2,2,1)=(0,0,-1)\); the first two coordinates
  force \(c=0\), while the third forces \(c=-1\).  Equivalently
  \(\widetilde\lambda_{02,\mathfrak b}\) annihilates every current
  boundary column and evaluates to \(-1\) on the target.  A formal new
  column \(A_B^2=(0,0,-1)^T\) closes this fixed matrix equation, but it
  defines a theorem only after the local functional, scalar-zero value,
  locality or wavefront data, and finite-window transport data are
  constructed.

  A companion-face table is not a primitive.  It changes the residual
  row by replacing the one-face CE source expression with the full
  signed CE boundary.  It is accepted exactly when the signed sum is zero
  and the truncation matrices preserve that zero sum in the inverse
  system.  The displayed Roos condition is precisely the obstruction to
  choosing the fixed-window primitives compatibly.  These are necessary
  and sufficient finite-window conditions; they do not by themselves
  construct the analytic Costello graph construction or any all-graph QME
  solution.
\end{proof}

\begin{prob}[Tate-coefficient lift of the descendant cochain isomorphism]\label{prob:tate-coefficient-descendant-lift}
  In the unweighted Tate pair, the diagnosis of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:tate-casimir-obstruction} pins the obstruction to the
  failure of the coefficient kernel; the resolution requires a
  category-level change of coefficient module.  The classical Tate
  obstruction is closed by the weighted Tate completion of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:wt-cotangent-lift}(a)--(d); the all-order quantum
  lift on the descendant sector is the quantum specialization of
  \ref{prob:formal-factorization-center} under the hypothesis of
  \ref{prob:weighted-rg-locality}.
\end{prob}

\begin{prop}[Open-line Weyl boundary product from midpoint kernel]\label{prop:conditional-boundary-product-normalization}
  For Weyl-ordered Hamiltonian boundary observables placed on the open line
  $\R$ in the reduced first-order open model
  $S_0=\int_\R\mathrm{Tr}(\phi_1\,d\phi_2)$, on two brane-time points
  $(t,t')\in\R^2_{\mathrm{brane}}$ (with $s$ reserved for the transverse
  $\R_s$ coordinate of $X$) and the zero-mode-removed midpoint
  propagator
  $$G(t,t')=\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{sgn}(t-t'),\qquad
    \langle\phi_1(t)\phi_2(t')\rangle=\hbar G(t,t'),\qquad
    \langle\phi_2(t)\phi_1(t')\rangle=-\hbar G(t,t'),$$
  the boundary product on $\bar A_\hbar$ is
  $$F\star G=m\circ\exp\!\left(\frac{\hbar}{2}P_{\partial}\right)(F\otimes G),
    \qquad P_{\partial}=\partial_{z_1}\otimes\partial_{z_2}
      -\partial_{z_2}\otimes\partial_{z_1}.$$
  Equivalently, every odd raw commutator coefficient is
  $\frac{2}{2^r r!}\hbar^r P^r(f,g)$. In particular the first and third
  coefficients are $\hbar P(f,g)$ and $\frac{\hbar^3}{24}P^3(f,g)$. This
  reduced open-line computation uses no Costello specialization. With these
  conventions, the Weyl normalization is determined by:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item the matrix Weyl algebra commutator
      $[(\phi_1)^i{}_j,(\phi_2)^k{}_l]=\hbar\delta^i_l\delta^k_j$;
    \item the zero-mode-removed midpoint propagator
      $G(\pm,\mp)=\pm\tfrac12$;
    \item the Baker--Campbell--Hausdorff determination of the cross-contraction
      weight $P/2$ on linear Hamiltonians;
    \item the linear-Hamiltonian commutator
      $[\widehat u,\widehat v]=\hbar(ad-bc)$ for $u=az_1+bz_2$,
      $v=cz_1+dz_2$.
  \end{enumerate}
\end{prop}

\begin{proof}
  Items (i)--(iv) form a normalization system. By (i)--(ii) the midpoint
  propagator at $t\neq t'$ has weight $\pm\hbar/2$. By (iv) the
  linear-Hamiltonian Weyl commutator is $\hbar(ad-bc)$, so the
  cross-contraction weight on linear Hamiltonians is exactly $1/2$, giving
  $P/2$. Hence by (iii) the BCH formula on the Heisenberg algebra of linear
  Hamiltonians fixes the cross-contraction kernel on the boundary as
  $P_{\partial}=P$ with weight $\hbar/2$.

  The all-order boundary product on $\bar A_\hbar$ is then determined by:
  (a) the local Weyl/BCH computation for the constant Poisson tensor,
  with Kontsevich deformation quantization \cite{kontsevich-dq} serving
  only as general context here;
  (b) Proposition~\ref{prop:moyal-monomial} together with item (iii) above;
  (c) the antisymmetrisation argument of
	  Theorem~\ref{thm:open-line-midpoint-graph-weights}.

  The "no additional factor of $N$, sign, or scalar moment-map term" clause
  is the content of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:capelli-renormalized-stable-trace}(iii): the Capelli
  contact term is absorbed into the $T\leftrightarrow J_N$ change of basis,
	  and the connected single-trace projection is the standard primitive
	  projection. Consequently all odd raw commutator coefficients are
  $\frac{2}{2^r r!}\hbar^r P^r(f,g)$, and for $r=1,3$ they are
  $\hbar P(f,g)$ and $\frac{\hbar^3}{24}P^3(f,g)$. The four primary cases
  (a)--(d) and a sweep of \(14641\) monomial pairs through order
  \(\hbar^{11}\) are exact-arithmetic verification checks for this
  coefficient formula. They are not a substitute for the external
  finite-\(N\) radial-parts input.
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}[Reduced open-line Wick theorem]\label{thm:open-line-midpoint-graph-weights}
  In the reduced open first-order line model
  $$S_0=\int_\R\mathrm{Tr}(\phi_1\,d\phi_2),$$
  on two brane-time points $(t,t')\in\R^2_{\mathrm{brane}}$ (with $s$
  reserved for the transverse $\R_s$ coordinate of $X$), choose the
  zero-mode-removed midpoint propagator
  $$G(t,t')=\frac12\operatorname{sgn}(t-t'),\qquad
    \langle\phi_1(t)\phi_2(t')\rangle=\hbar G(t,t'),\quad
    \langle\phi_2(t)\phi_1(t')\rangle=-\hbar G(t,t').$$
  For ordered insertions \(t>t'\), let \(W_r^>(f,g)\) be the contribution
  of the graph with exactly \(r\) cross-contractions between the two
  Weyl-ordered boundary vertices.  Let \(W_r^<(f,g)\) be the reversed
  ordering contribution.  Then
  \[
    W_r^>(f,g)=\frac1{r!}\left(\frac{\hbar}{2}\right)^rP^r(f,g),
    \qquad
    W_r^<(f,g)=\frac1{r!}\left(-\frac{\hbar}{2}\right)^rP^r(f,g).
  \]
  Consequently the reduced open-line commutator is
  \[
    [f,g]_{\mathrm{Wick}}
      =
      \sum_{\substack{r\geq1\\ r\ \mathrm{odd}}}
      \frac{2}{2^r r!}\hbar^rP^r(f,g),
  \]
  and all even orders vanish.  For monomials
  \(f=z_1^kz_2^l\), \(g=z_1^mz_2^n\),
  \[
    P^r(f,g)=
    \sum_{a=0}^r(-1)^a\binom ra
    (k)_{r-a}(l)_a(m)_a(n)_{r-a}
    z_1^{k+m-r}z_2^{l+n-r}.
  \]
  In particular the one-edge and three-edge commutator contributions are
  \[
    \hbar P(f,g),\qquad \frac{\hbar^3}{24}P^3(f,g).
  \]
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
  The midpoint value $G(+,-)=1/2$ assigns one copy of
  $$P=\partial_{z_1}\otimes\partial_{z_2}
    -\partial_{z_2}\otimes\partial_{z_1}$$
  to each cross-contraction, with weight \(\hbar/2\).  With Weyl vertices
  there are no self-contractions.  The graph with \(r\) identical
  cross-edges has automorphism group \(S_r\), so the ordered contribution
  is
  \[
    W_r^>(f,g)=\frac1{r!}\left(\frac{\hbar}{2}\right)^rP^r(f,g).
  \]
  Reversing the order changes the midpoint propagator from \(1/2\) to
  \(-1/2\), giving the displayed formula for \(W_r^<\).  Hence
  \[
    W_r^>(f,g)-W_r^<(f,g)
      =\frac{1-(-1)^r}{2^r r!}\hbar^rP^r(f,g),
  \]
  which is zero for even \(r\) and \(2\hbar^rP^r(f,g)/(2^r r!)\) for odd
  \(r\).

  The monomial formula is the expansion of
  \(P^r=(\partial_{z_1}\otimes\partial_{z_2}
  -\partial_{z_2}\otimes\partial_{z_1})^r\) by the binomial theorem:
  the summand with \(a\) copies of
  \(-\partial_{z_2}\otimes\partial_{z_1}\) contributes the sign
  \((-1)^a\), the binomial coefficient \(\binom ra\), and the four
  falling factorials shown above.  The values at \(r=1\) and \(r=3\)
  give \(\hbar P(f,g)\) and \(\hbar^3P^3(f,g)/24\).
\end{proof}

\begin{rmk}
  Theorem~\ref{thm:open-line-midpoint-graph-weights} is a computation in
  the reduced open-line Weyl model. It is not the Costello closed-open graph
  theorem. The latter still requires an elliptic parent or dimensional
  reduction, a brane-defect condition, finite-scale gauge fixing, the induced
  bulk-to-defect propagator, orientations and measures on configuration spaces,
  counterterms, anomaly cancellation, and compatibility with the quantum
  Hamiltonian reduction.
\end{rmk}

\subsubsection{Third-order target for Costello's graph problem}

The order-\(\hbar^1\) Moyal coefficient \(P\) is fixed by the
linear-Hamiltonian Heisenberg commutator and gives the target
\(\hbar(kn-lm)z_1^{k+m-1}z_2^{l+n-1}\).  At order \(\hbar^3\) the
Moyal target is \(P^3/24\); a Costello-graph realisation of the same
coefficient would supply a separate trivalent boundary contraction
diagram.  Whether the two answers match is the
content of this subsection.

For monomials $f=z_1^kz_2^l$ and $g=z_1^mz_2^n$, the third-order Moyal
coefficient from Proposition~\ref{prop:moyal-monomial} is
$$P^3(f,g)=
  \sum_{a=0}^3(-1)^a\binom{3}{a}
  (k)_{3-a}(l)_a(m)_a(n)_{3-a}
  z_1^{k+m-3}z_2^{l+n-3},$$
where $(r)_s=r(r-1)\cdots(r-s+1)$ and $(r)_0=1$. Therefore
$$[f,g]_*=\hbar(kn-lm)z_1^{k+m-1}z_2^{l+n-1}
  +\frac{\hbar^3}{24}P^3(f,g)+O(\hbar^5).$$
The exact-arithmetic verification checks this first-order coefficient, the
vanishing of the second-order coefficient in the raw commutator, the displayed
third-order formula, and the star-commutator normalization on every monomial
pair with exponents between \(0\) and \(10\) and commutator orders through
\(11\).

This algebraic computation is an observable-product target, not an effective
action computation. In Costello's formalism the connected effective action and
the product/OPE of quantum observables are related but distinct. For two
boundary observables $F,G$, the finite-scale free-field model has the schematic
product
$$F\star_L G=m\circ\exp(\hbar\partial_{P_{\partial,L}})(F\otimes G),$$
where $P_{\partial,L}$ is the boundary or brane-defect propagator induced by the
chosen kernel. The connected single-trace part is the primitive trace
component extracted from this product. In this convention the order in $\hbar$ is
the number of boundary cross-contractions; it is not the loop order of the
connected effective interaction.

Thus there are two equivalent normalizations to keep separate. In Costello's
observable-product notation one asks for the boundary contraction kernel
$$P_{\partial,L}\leadsto \frac12P$$
on degree-zero Hamiltonians, so that
$$m\circ\exp(\hbar\partial_{P_{\partial,L}})
  \leadsto m\circ\exp\left(\frac{\hbar}{2}P\right),$$
the Weyl/Moyal product used above. The ordered product has labeled inputs
$(f,g)$; its $r$ identical cross-contractions contribute the denominator $r!$.
No additional division by interchanging the two boundary vertices is made in
the ordered product. The reverse order is the separate product $g*f$ whose
subtraction forms the commutator.

The graphs $\Gamma_{3a}$ and $\Gamma_{3b}$ of Figure~\ref{fig:gamma3} record the
displayed order-$\hbar^3$ boundary contraction types whose tensor target is
$P^3(f,g)$. Each cross-contraction contributes one copy of the constant Poisson
bivector $P$. Their reduced open-line weights and automorphism factors are
computed above. Their realization as Costello brane-defect graphs still
requires the sector specialization, propagator, counterterm, and anomaly
checks in the problem below.

\begin{thm}[Costello first/third normalization recognition criterion]\label{thm:first-third-costello-normalizations}
  Assume the Hamiltonian specialization data of
  Problem~\ref{prob:analytic-graph-realization} have been supplied for a
  Costello-type closed-open BV theory, and assume that the induced
  brane-defect observable product restricts on degree-zero Hamiltonians to the
  midpoint kernel $P/2$ of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:open-line-midpoint-graph-weights}. Assume further that
  at order $\hbar^3$ every connected local term not represented by the
  three-parallel-edge cross-contraction has been shown to vanish, to be
  counterterm-exact, or to land in the discarded scalar trace. Then the first-
  and third-order Costello boundary commutator coefficients are determined by
  the Weyl/Moyal coefficients:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item the connected one-propagator brane-defect ribbon graph carries
      coefficient $\hbar(kn-lm)z_1^{k+m-1}z_2^{l+n-1}$ for monomial inputs
      $f=z_1^kz_2^l$, $g=z_1^mz_2^n$;
    \item the surviving three-propagator contribution carries coefficient
      $\frac{\hbar^3}{24}P^3(f,g)$;
    \item any finite counterterm ambiguity that changes the first or third
      coefficient is fixed by matching the Moyal coefficients $1$ and
      $1/24$ at these orders.  All-order Costello uniqueness lies
      outside this two-order test.
  \end{enumerate}
  Equivalently, the Weyl/Moyal coefficient test for a Costello specialization
  at these orders holds precisely
  when the specialization supplies the above data and the normalization
  defects
  \[
    \mathfrak n_1
    =
    P_{\partial,L}|_{\mathrm{Ham}^0}-\frac12P,
  \]
  \[
    \mathfrak n_3
    =
    \left[
      \operatorname{ObsProd}^{(3)}_{\mathrm{Costello}}(f,g)
      -\frac{\hbar^3}{24}P^3(f,g)
    \right]_{\mathrm{Ham}^0,\mathrm{conn},\mathrm{red}}
  \]
  vanish, together with the scalar-contact, reduced-kernel, and
  \(\theta_3\) graph-row obstructions.  The obstruction tuple is
  \[
    \mathfrak O_{\mathrm{Cost}}^{1,3}
    =
    (\mathfrak o_{\mathrm{Cas}},
     \hbar N[\bar c]\ \text{or}\ o_{\sigma,w}^{(r)},
     [\operatorname{Ob}^{\mathrm{red}}_{w,\partial,\hbar}],
     \mathfrak O_{\theta_3},
     \mathfrak n_1,
     \mathfrak n_3).
  \]
  Absent this datum, the theorem proved in
  Theorem~\ref{thm:open-line-midpoint-graph-weights} is the reduced
  open-line Wick normalization, not a Costello heat-kernel graph
  realization.
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
  Under the stated hypotheses, the degree-zero boundary observable product is
  the reduced open-line Weyl product of
  Proposition~\ref{prop:conditional-boundary-product-normalization}. Thus each
  boundary cross-contraction contributes the operator $P$ with weight
  $\hbar/2$.
  Here the \(S_3\) count treats the three edges as copies of the aggregate
  bivector \(P=A-B\).  After expanding \(P^3\), a mixed orientation class
  has residual automorphism \(S_a\times S_{3-a}\) and binomial coefficient
  \(\binom{3}{a}\). These are equivalent normalizations:
  \(\binom{3}{a}/3!=1/(a!(3-a)!)\); the aggregate \(S_3\) denominator
  and the residual orientation-class denominator are alternative
  descriptions of the same total automorphism count.

  Item (i): one edge, no edge automorphisms, weight $\hbar P/2$ for the
  ordered product (Theorem~\ref{thm:open-line-midpoint-graph-weights});
  antisymmetrisation gives
  $$\hbar P(f,g)=\hbar(kn-lm)z_1^{k+m-1}z_2^{l+n-1}$$
  on monomials.

  Item (ii): for three parallel edges between the two labelled boundary
  observables, the edge automorphism group is $S_3$. The ordered weight is
  $$\hbar^3\frac1{3!}\left(\frac12\right)^3P^3(f,g)
    =\frac{\hbar^3}{48}P^3(f,g),$$
  and antisymmetrisation doubles it to $\hbar^3P^3(f,g)/24$. The hypotheses
  exclude the remaining connected local terms at the same order.

  Item (iii): a finite local counterterm that changes the one- or three-edge
  boundary product changes the coefficient of $P$ or $P^3$. The
  linear-Hamiltonian Heisenberg commutator fixes the coefficient of $P$ to
  $1$, and Proposition~\ref{prop:moyal-monomial} fixes the coefficient of
  $P^3$ to $1/24$ in the raw commutator. Thus matching these two coefficients
  removes precisely the counterterm freedom visible at these orders.  The
  displayed defect tuple is a restatement of the hypotheses needed to promote
  this reduced computation to a Costello graph realization: the first defect fixes
  the brane-restricted propagator, the third-order defect fixes the
  connected reduced product, and the remaining entries are the scalar,
  reduced-kernel, and \(\theta_3\) graph-row conditions developed in
  Problem~\ref{prob:quantum-p0-operation-realization}.
\end{proof}

\begin{rmk}
  In the reduced open-line model the Weyl parameter $\hbar$ counts boundary
  cross-contractions. If a Costello specialization restricts to this free
  boundary kernel, then its observable-product expansion must be read with this
  edge-counting convention on degree-zero Hamiltonians. Without the
  specialization data of Problem~\ref{prob:analytic-graph-realization}, the
  statement is only the formal Weyl/Moyal normalization test above.
\end{rmk}

\begin{prob}[Costello first/third graph-normalization obstruction datum]\label{prob:first-third-graph-normalizations}
  Work inside the observable product supplied by
  Statement~\ref{stmt:costello-bv-construction}, after solving the Hamiltonian
  specialization data of Problem~\ref{prob:analytic-graph-realization}. The
  first graph fixes the normalization; the third graph tests whether the
  Costello specialization really lands on the Weyl/Moyal product rather than on
  another deformation quantization convention. The analytic problem is reduced
  to the following checks:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item identify the connected one-propagator brane-defect ribbon graph whose
      tensor contraction is $P(f,g)$, and prove that its antisymmetrised
      boundary product gives the raw coefficient
      $\hbar P(f,g)$;
    \item fix the sign, automorphism factor, boundary orientation, and trace
      normalization in this first-order graph so that the coefficient is
      $\hbar(kn-lm)z_1^{k+m-1}z_2^{l+n-1}$ on monomials;
    \item identify the connected three-propagator brane-defect ribbon graphs
      whose tensor contraction is $P^3(f,g)$;
	    \item prove that every other connected order-$\hbar^3$ contribution in the
	      Hamiltonian sector vanishes, is counterterm-exact, or lands in the
	      discarded scalar trace;
	    \item for every term declared counterterm-exact in the preceding item,
	      exhibit compatible local counterterm representatives
	      \(C_{\Gamma,w}^{w_0}(\epsilon)\in
	      \mathcal O_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal E_w^{w_0})\), prove Costello
	      locality and RG compatibility, and verify that their restriction to
	      degree-zero Hamiltonians does not alter the fixed one-edge coefficient
	      except by the allowed Moyal normalization choice;
	    \item compute the graph weight and automorphism factor after the
	      first-order normalization and show that the coefficient is
	      $\frac1{24}$;
	    \item check that connected large-$N$ extraction introduces no additional
	      factor of $N$, sign, or scalar moment-map term;
	    \item verify that the same observable product, counterterm subtraction,
	      and connected extraction commute with the principal-part current
	      extension of
	      Theorem~\ref{thm:reduced-principal-part-boundary-current} on the
	      reduced target. If the claim is made before de Rham-current
	      contraction, identify the additional unreduced homotopies of
	      Remark~\ref{rmk:unreduced-lift-obstruction}. In particular the degree-zero
	      Moyal product must act on the cotangent current summand through the
	      principal-part coadjoint action, not through polynomial one-\(\psi\)
	      descendants.
	  \end{enumerate}
	  The target normalization is the pair of algebraic coefficients
  $$\hbar P(f,g),\qquad \frac{\hbar^3}{24}P^3(f,g)$$
  in the Weyl/Moyal commutator.
  Theorem~\ref{thm:first-third-costello-normalizations} records the
  implication once the Costello specialization supplies these checks.
\end{prob}

\begin{rmk}
  In characteristic zero, $GL_N$ is linearly reductive on rational
  representations. Therefore taking $GL_N$-invariants is exact, and
  $$H\bigl((\mc R_N)^{GL_N},Q\bigr)\cong H(\mc R_N,Q)^{GL_N}.$$
  This justifies the degree-zero rational invariant calculation used above.
  It does not identify that calculation with the full Lie-algebra
  Chevalley--Eilenberg BRST cohomology of \(\lie{gl}_N\).  A stack-level
  presentation remembers stabilizers, including the trivially acting scalar
  \(B\mathbb G_m\); those ghost and stabilizer classes are outside the
  Hamiltonian trace sector computed here.  Linearly reductive exactness
  identifies derived algebraic invariants of functions with ordinary
  invariants in this setting.
\end{rmk}

\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$\mathcal{W}_\infty[\lambda]/E_\infty$}{W-infty[lambda]/E-infty} admissible endpoint}\label{ssec:w-infinity-endpoint}

On the admissible locus defined by hypotheses (H1)--(H4) below, the
$\mathcal W_N\to\mathcal W_\infty[\lambda]$ family of two-parameter
$\mathcal W$-algebras converges to an $E_\infty$-algebra carrying the
formal-Darboux stalk identification of
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-local}.  The locus is named
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}\subset\{(N,k,\lambda)\}$; the limit
$\lim_{N\to\infty}A^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\partial,N}|_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}}$
is taken in the Yamada admissible-weight topology.  The statement is
\emph{conditional}: each of the four hypotheses corresponds to a
proved structural input in the cited literature whose composite
gives the named limit.

\paragraph{The four hypotheses.}
\begin{description}
  \item[(H1) Prochazka triangular truncation
    \cite{prochazka-w-infinity}.]  The two-parameter algebra
    $\mathcal W_\infty[\mu,\lambda]$ admits a quadratic basis of
    primary generators $W_s$ for spin $s\geq 2$, with structure
    constants polynomial in $(\lambda,c)$ that organise into a
    triangular truncation pattern: the level-$s$ closure ideal
    $\mathcal I_s\subset\mathcal W_\infty[\mu,\lambda]$ is generated
    by the spin-$s$ primaries, and the quotient is
    $\mathcal W_s[\lambda]\simeq\mathcal W_N$ at the integer
    truncation $\lambda=N$.  At the family level this is the
    triangular spectral data on which the $N\to\infty$ direction
    limit is defined.
  \item[(H2) Creutzig--Kanade--Linshaw parafermion compatibility
    \cite{creutzig-kanade-linshaw-coset}.]  The level-rank coset
    realisation
    $\mathcal W_N\simeq\bigl(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_N{}_k
      \otimes\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_N{}_1\bigr)/\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_N{}_{k+1}$
    is compatible with the $N\to\infty$ truncation of (H1) at the
    level of generalised parafermion cosets.  The compatibility
    statement is a quasi-isomorphism of vertex algebras in admissible
    weight; outside admissible weight the comparison fails.
  \item[(H3) Pope--Romans--Shen / Bakas closed-form OPE
    \cites{pope-romans-shen,bakas-w-infinity}.]  The OPE of
    $\mathcal W_\infty[\lambda]$ generators
    $W_s\cdot W_t$ admits a closed-form spin-summation kernel
    parametrised by $\lambda$, derived in the $w_\infty$ contraction
    limit and quantum-corrected to $\mathcal W_\infty[\lambda]$.  The
    structure constants compatible with (H1)--(H2) are recorded in
    the closed-form generating function.
  \item[(H4) Yamada admissible-weight window
    \cite{arakawa-admissible-w}.]  The admissible-weight locus
    $\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}$ for $\mathcal W_N$ at level $k$ is
    Arakawa's admissible-weight window for the affine vertex algebra
    at level $k$, whose $\mathcal W_N$-image carries a
    finite-dimensional space of conformal blocks in each spin window.
    The window is the topology in which the $N\to\infty$ limit is
    taken; the limit is a topological vector-space limit indexed by
    spin truncation.
\end{description}

\begin{thm}[Conditional $\mathcal W_\infty[\lambda]/E_\infty$ admissible
  endpoint]\label{thm:w-infty-e-infty-admissible}
  Assume (H1)--(H4).  On the admissible locus
  $\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}$, the formal-Darboux stalk identification
  of Theorem~\ref{thm:main-local} extends to a limit
  identification
  \[
    \lim_{N\to\infty}A^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\partial,N}\Big|_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}}
      \;\simeq\;
      C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}\bigl(A_b,A_b\bigr)\Big|_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}}
  \]
  as $E_\infty$-algebras over the Yamada window, with chart the
  CE/PV dictionary
  \[
    c_f \;\longmapsto\; \theta_f,\qquad
    u_f \;\longmapsto\; J(f)\;=\;\operatorname{Tr} f(\phi_1,\phi_2),
  \]
  and Capelli scalar transported to the higher-spin central
  extension of $w_\infty$:
  \[
    \hbar N[\bar c]\;=\;\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}\Big|_{J(f)}
    \quad\rightsquigarrow\quad
    c_{w_\infty}(\lambda)\;=\;\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}\Big|_{J_{\mathcal W_\infty}(f)},
  \]
  where $c_{w_\infty}(\lambda)$ is the Pope--Romans--Shen central
  parameter at the spin-tower limit and $J_{\mathcal W_\infty}$ the
  trace-tower limit of $J$ along the admissible truncation.
\end{thm}

The load-bearing identification is the trace map: the
$\mathcal W_N$-trace
$J_N(f)=\operatorname{Tr} f(\phi_1,\phi_2)$ becomes, under the (H1)--(H4)
admissible truncation, the $\mathcal W_\infty[\lambda]$-trace at
spin-tower limit; the Capelli scalar is transported to the
higher-spin central charge of $w_\infty$.  The CE/PV dictionary
$c_f\mapsto\theta_f$, $u_f\mapsto J(f)$ is the formal Darboux
chart; on $\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}$ the chart is preserved by the
spin-tower limit.

\paragraph{Spin-$\leq 8$ OPE checks as evidence.}  Pope--Romans--Shen
and Bakas verify the closed-form spin-summation kernel by direct
computation through spin~$8$.  Per the seventeen-site catalogue
(row~14): \emph{spin-$\leq 8$ OPE checks are evidence within
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}$}, not proof of $E_\infty$.  The
$E_\infty$-algebra structure of
Theorem~\ref{thm:w-infty-e-infty-admissible} is the conditional
content of (H1)--(H4); the spin-tower convergence to that
$E_\infty$-structure is hypothesis (H4).

\paragraph{Open obligations.}  Three open obligations sharpen the
admissible endpoint to an unconditional theorem.

\begin{enumerate}
  \item[(O1)] \emph{Closed-form OPE on $\Lambda_{\mathrm{adm}}$.}
    Prove (H3) directly on the admissible locus, supplying a
    closed-form generating function for the structure constants of
    $\mathcal W_\infty[\lambda]$ valid at all spins compatible with
    the (H1)--(H2) truncation pattern.  Pope--Romans--Shen and
    Bakas provide the spin-$\leq 8$ check; the residual obligation
    is the all-spin closed form.
  \item[(O2)] \emph{Spin-tower convergence in the Yamada window.}
    Prove (H4) as a topological convergence theorem: identify the
    space of conformal blocks at admissible weight as a
    Fr\'echet-type vector space and prove that the $N\to\infty$
    limit of the (H1) triangular truncation converges in this
    topology.  Outside admissible weight the convergence fails.
  \item[(O3)] \emph{Costello--Paquette twisted-holography
    matched-conventions comparison.}  Twisted holography
    \cite{costello-paquette-twisted-holography} identifies a
    $\mathcal W_\infty$-bulk algebra with a chiral Hochschild
    cohomology of a boundary algebra in a related but distinct
    convention layer.  The matched-conventions transfer of
    Theorem~\ref{thm:matched-conventions-transfer} delivers the
    formal-Darboux stalk to the twisted-holography
    $\mathcal W_\infty$ at the cost of an obstruction class
    $\operatorname{Ob}_{\mathrm{CP}}\in\mathfrak K_T$ in the
    cross-volume convention contract; identify
    $\operatorname{Ob}_{\mathrm{CP}}$ explicitly to make the
    comparison structural.
\end{enumerate}

The admissible endpoint of
Theorem~\ref{thm:w-infty-e-infty-admissible}, conditional on
(H1)--(H4) and the open obligations (O1)--(O3), is the
$\mathcal W$-algebra fibre of the formal-Darboux stalk; it forces
the frontier problems of the next section.

\section{The frontier}\label{sec:frontier}

The formal-Darboux stalk theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:main-local}) is
the tangent vector at one Dirac brane vacuum to a global
identification
\[
  Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{E_d^{\mathrm{ch}}}\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}_\partial\bigr)
    \;\simeq\;
    C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_b,A_b),
    \qquad
    \mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}_\partial=\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d(\mathcal{A})\bigl|_{\partial X^{\mathrm{KN}}},
\]
of the closed sector with the chiral Hochschild cohomology of the
boundary algebra at a chosen brane vacuum $b$ on the Kato--Nakayama
log boundary $\partial X^{\mathrm{KN}}$.  Three open problems are
forced by the formal-Darboux stalk and now well-posed: the chiral
$E_d$ Deligne theorem at $d\geq 2$
(\S\ref{ssec:open-chiral-Ed-Deligne}); the
Hall--Drinfeld--Borcherds comparison spectral sequence on compact
non-toric CY$_3$ (\S\ref{ssec:open-hall-drinfeld-borcherds}); and
the operator-level lift of the modular line on $K3\times E$
(\S\ref{ssec:open-modular-lift}).  Three frontier inputs anchor
these problems: MNOP / framing on $S^3$
(\S\ref{ssec:frontier-mnop-framing}), the Hall--Drinfeld
realisation of the chiral specialisation on compact Calabi--Yau
threefolds (\S\ref{ssec:frontier-hall-drinfeld}), and the
gravitational lift of the Igusa cusp form $\Phi_{10}=\chi_{10}$
(\S\ref{ssec:frontier-phi10-gravitational}).  The cross-volume
firewall (Appendix~\ref{app:convention-contract};
Theorem~\ref{thm:cross-volume-firewall};
Theorem~\ref{thm:matched-conventions-transfer}) records the
matched-conventions acceptance class
$\operatorname{Ob}_{\mathrm{UKD}}(\mathfrak T)$ which the
formal-Darboux stalk delivers to any compact-target theorem; the
three open problems are the targets at which
$\operatorname{Ob}_{\mathrm{UKD}}$ becomes the obstruction to the
global identification.  Volume
\texttt{frontier\_mnop\_framing\_volume.tex} develops the
compact-CY$_3$ scalar and Fourier--Mukai data (quintic DT/PT
factorisation, $S^3$ framing, chiral-volume finite evidence,
$K3\times E$ Fourier--Mukai partner, Gopakumar--Vafa closure) used
below as comparison anchors.

\subsection{Open problem: chiral
  \texorpdfstring{$E_d$}{Ed} Deligne conjecture at
  \texorpdfstring{$d\geq 2$}{d>=2}}\label{ssec:open-chiral-Ed-Deligne}

The Deligne conjecture in its $E_d$-form asserts an equivalence
\[
  Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{E_d}(A)
    \;\simeq\;
    C^\bullet_{E_d}(A,A)
\]
between the derived $E_d$-centre of an $E_d$-algebra $A$ and the
$E_d$-Hochschild cochain complex.  At $d=1$ the topological form
is Lurie \cite{lurie-ha}*{Thm.~5.3.1.14} (after Tamarkin); the
chiral form at $d=1$ on a curve $C$ is Beilinson--Drinfeld
\cite{beilinson-drinfeld-chiral}*{\S 4} with the chiral centre
$Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)\simeq C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}(A,A)$
realised through the chiral envelope and chiral cobar; the
factorization-algebra reformulation is Costello--Gwilliam
\cite{costello-gwilliam}*{Vol.~I \S 5--6}.  At $d\geq 2$ the
topological $E_d$-centre is constructed by Lurie
\cite{lurie-ha}*{\S 5.3} via $E_d$-module categories; the chiral
upgrade requires three constructions not present in the
literature.

\begin{enumerate}
  \item \emph{A chiral $E_d$-operad on $\C^d$.}  The little
    holomorphic disks operad $E_d^{\mathrm{ch}}$ on $\C^d$ must
    carry the holomorphic factorization structure of
    Beilinson--Drinfeld extended from curves to higher-dimensional
    affine space.  Costello--Gwilliam
    \cite{costello-gwilliam,costello-gwilliam-vol2} establish
    holomorphic factorization algebras on $\C^d$ as a well-defined
    symmetric-monoidal $\infty$-category; a chiral $E_d$-operad
    action on this category, refining the topological $E_d$-action
    via right $\overline\partial$-modules, is the missing object.
  \item \emph{A centre-of-module-category construction in the
    chiral setting.}  At $d=1$ the chiral centre
    $Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)$ is the endomorphism object
    in chiral $A$-modules
    (\cite{beilinson-drinfeld-chiral}*{\S 3.4}); at $d\geq 2$ the
    analogous construction must take a chiral $E_d$-algebra to its
    derived chiral $E_d$-centre, compatibly with factorization
    homology.
  \item \emph{Compatibility with factorization homology
    $\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ch}}_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}$.}  The two-stage
    functor $\Phi_d=\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ch}}_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}\circ
    \Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d$ of the master architecture
    (Lurie--Ayala--Francis factorization homology
    \cite{lurie-ha}*{\S 5.5}) must intertwine the chiral
    $E_d$-centre on its source with the chiral Hochschild centre
    on its target.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{conj}[Chiral $E_d$ Deligne]\label{conj:chiral-Ed-deligne}
  Let $\mathcal A$ be a chiral $E_d^{\mathrm{ch}}$-algebra on $\C^d$
  in the sense of (1).  There is a natural equivalence
  \[
    Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{E_d^{\mathrm{ch}}}(\mathcal A)
    \;\simeq\;
    C^\bullet_{E_d^{\mathrm{ch}}}(\mathcal A,\mathcal A)
  \]
  in chiral $E_{d+1}^{\mathrm{ch}}$-algebras, lifting the
  topological Deligne conjecture of Lurie
  \cite{lurie-ha}*{Thm.~5.3.1.14} along the chiral refinement.
\end{conj}

The $d=1$ case is proved (Beilinson--Drinfeld
\cite{beilinson-drinfeld-chiral}; Costello--Gwilliam
\cite{costello-gwilliam}*{Vol.~I}).  At $d\geq 2$ partial results
exist for the topological $E_d$-centre
(\cite{lurie-ha}*{\S 5.3.1}) and for chiral algebras on smooth
schemes (Hennion's chiral Lie algebroids; Francis' factorisable
co-operads); the chiral $E_d$-operad on $\C^d$ at $d\geq 2$ in the
sense of~(1) is open.  The formal-Darboux stalk theorem of this
manuscript (Theorem~\ref{thm:main-local}) is the $d=2$
formal-local instance with $\mathcal A$ the holomorphic $E_2$
factorization algebra
$\Omega^{0,\bullet}_c(B)\widehat\otimes\mathfrak g$,
$\mathfrak g=\mathfrak h\ltimes\mathfrak h^\vee_{\mathrm{cont}}[1]$,
$\mathfrak h=\C[[z_1,z_2]]/\C$; the stalk identifies the formal
coordinate of the $d=2$ chiral $E_d$-centre with the trace chart
$J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}\,f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}$.

\subsection{Open problem: Hall--Drinfeld--Borcherds comparison
  spectral sequence on compact non-toric
  CY$_3$}\label{ssec:open-hall-drinfeld-borcherds}

On $K3\times E$ five comparison anchors carry distinct numerical
weights:
\[
  \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E)=0,\qquad
  \kappa^{\mathrm{Hodge}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3\times E)=0,
\]
\[
  \kappa^{\mathrm{Heis}}_{\mathrm{ch}}=3,\qquad
  \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_{10})=5,\qquad
  \mathrm{rk}\,\widetilde H^*(K3,\Z)=24,
\]
the categorical Euler invariant
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}=\chi(\mathcal O_{K3\times E})$, the chiral
Hodge supertrace
$\kappa^{\mathrm{Hodge}}_{\mathrm{ch}}=\chi(\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_2(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{Hodge}}))$,
the chiral Heisenberg weight
$\kappa^{\mathrm{Heis}}_{\mathrm{ch}}$ of the boundary Heisenberg
factor, the Borcherds--Kac--Moody (BKM) denominator weight
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_{10})=5$ recording the lift exponent
of $\Phi_{10}=\chi_{10}$ in the Gritsenko paramodular row, and the
extended Mukai-lattice rank of $K3$.  These five numbers are five
distinct construction layers, not one invariant
(Appendix~\ref{app:convention-contract}, Vol.~III
matched-conventions data;
\texttt{frontier\_mnop\_framing\_volume.tex}, \S\ref{sec:fm-k3e}).

\begin{conj}[Hall--Drinfeld--Borcherds comparison spectral
  sequence on compact non-toric
  CY$_3$]\label{conj:hall-drinfeld-borcherds-ss}
  On a compact non-toric Calabi--Yau threefold $X$ with chosen
  Hall--Drinfeld--Borcherds matched-conventions datum (Vol.~III;
  convention contract Appendix~\ref{app:convention-contract},
  Theorem~\ref{thm:matched-conventions-transfer}), the five
  comparison weights organise into the $E_2$-page of a spectral
  sequence
  \[
    E_2^{p,q}(X)
    \;=\;
    \bigl(\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}},\,\kappa^{\mathrm{Hodge}}_{\mathrm{ch}},\,
    \kappa^{\mathrm{Heis}}_{\mathrm{ch}},\,
    \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}},\,\kappa_{\mathrm{fibre}}\bigr)(X)
    \;\Longrightarrow\;
    \kappa(X)
  \]
  whose differentials $d_r\colon E_r^{p,q}\to E_r^{p+r,q-r+1}$ are
  the Hall--Borcherds comparison residuals tracked by the
  matched-conventions obstruction
  $\operatorname{ob}_{\mathrm{Igusa/BKM}}\oplus
  \operatorname{ob}_{\mathrm{VolIII}}$ of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:cross-volume-firewall}, and whose abutment
  $\kappa(X)$ is a single $\kappa$-invariant of $X$.  On
  $X=K3\times E$ the $E_2$-page reads $(0,0,3,5,24)$, and
  convergence to $\kappa(K3\times E)$ is the conjectural content.
\end{conj}

The construction sketch on $K3\times E$ is the Fourier--Mukai
partner data of \texttt{frontier\_mnop\_framing\_volume.tex},
\S\ref{sec:fm-k3e}: under the coprimality hypothesis~$(\star)$,
the universal sheaf gives a derived equivalence
$D^b(K3)\simeq D^b(M)$ with $M$ a $K3$ partner; the Mukai-lattice
side feeds the $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}=0$, fibre Euler $=2$, and
extended-rank $=24$ entries through K\"unneth, and the
Heisenberg/BKM entries arrive through the chiral specialisation
$\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_2$ along $K3$ paired with the Borcherds product
realisation of $\Phi_{10}$ on $E$.  No equality among the five
numbers follows from the Fourier--Mukai theorem alone
(\texttt{frontier\_mnop\_framing\_volume.tex}, \S\ref{sec:fm-k3e},
distinct $\kappa$ values); the spectral-sequence interpretation
is the conjectural content; convergence on a generic compact
non-toric CY$_3$ requires the Hall--Drinfeld--Borcherds
construction of Vol.~III and the matched-conventions firewall of
Theorem~\ref{thm:matched-conventions-transfer}.

\subsection{Open problem: operator-level lift of the modular line
  on \texorpdfstring{$K3\times E$}{K3 x E}}\label{ssec:open-modular-lift}

The Capelli scalar
$\hbar N[\bar c]=\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}|_{J(f)}$ of
\S\ref{subsec:scalar-anomaly} is the local datum: the projective
curvature of the modular line bundle on the period domain
$\overline{\mathcal A}_g$, evaluated at the trace generator.  At
$g=2$ the level-$2$ section of $\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}$ is the
Igusa cusp form $\Phi_{10}=\chi_{10}$, and
$\Delta_5=\Phi_{10}^{1/2}$ is its square-root branch governing the
paramodular Borcherds product.  The candidate operator carrying
the modular line is the bulk module
$C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_b,A_b)$ at the brane vacuum of the
$K3\times E$ holomorphic-topological theory, twisted by
$\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}$ at level~$2$.

\begin{conj}[Operator-level lift of $\Phi_{10}$]\label{conj:operator-modular-lift}
  Let $A_b$ be the boundary algebra at a Dirac brane vacuum $b$ on
  $\partial(K3\times E)^{\mathrm{KN}}$, the Kato--Nakayama log
  boundary of $K3\times E$.  The bulk module
  $C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_b,A_b)$ carries a canonical lift of
  the level-$2$ projective curvature
  $\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}\bigl|_{\overline{\mathcal A}_2}$ to an
  operator-valued cocycle
  $\widehat\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}^{\,(2)}$ whose protected
  scalar trace
  $\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}_\partial}\bigl(\widehat\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}^{\,(2)}\bigr)$
  equals $\Phi_{10}=\chi_{10}$ at every paramodular CM point.  The
  Hall--Borcherds comparison residual of
  Conjecture~\ref{conj:hall-drinfeld-borcherds-ss}, restricted to
  the $K3\times E$ five-layer page, is the obstruction class to
  this lift.
\end{conj}

The construction problem is the lift of the projective curvature
class $\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}$ from the period domain to an
operator on the chiral Hochschild bulk module: the local datum of
\S\ref{subsec:scalar-anomaly} supplies the fibre, the modular line
bundle on $\overline{\mathcal A}_g$ supplies the global structure,
and the Hall--Borcherds residual identifies the obstruction.  The
level-$2$ section is forced to be $\Phi_{10}$ by the Gritsenko
paramodular row and the Borcherds product realisation
(cross-volume reference: the Igusa cusp form volume); the operator
lift is the construction problem on the bulk side.  The
cross-volume firewall theorem
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cross-volume-firewall}; matched-conventions
class $\operatorname{ob}_{\mathrm{Igusa/BKM}}$) records the data
which must be supplied for the lift to descend from the bulk
module to a section of the modular line bundle.  $\Delta_5$ is the
level-$2$ modular section of $\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}$; the
operator-level lift is the open construction problem.

\subsection{Frontier input: MNOP and framing on
  \texorpdfstring{$S^3$}{S3}}\label{ssec:frontier-mnop-framing}

On a compact Calabi--Yau threefold $X$ the Donaldson--Thomas (DT),
stable-pair Pandharipande--Thomas (PT), and Gromov--Witten (GW)
generating series carry a priori distinct chain-level trace data;
the Maulik--Nekrasov--Okounkov--Pandharipande (MNOP) package
asserts a single chain-level automorphism
$\sigma_X\in\operatorname{Aut}_{E_2}(Z_{E_2}(\mathcal F_X))$ of
the $E_2$-centre of
$\mathcal F_X=\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_3(\operatorname{Perf}(X))$
implementing the substitution $-q=e^{iu}$ on
$(\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{DT}},\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{PT}},
\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{GW}})$.  The proven half on the smooth
quintic is the DT/PT factorisation by the MacMahon power off the
degree-zero locus
(\texttt{frontier\_mnop\_framing\_volume.tex},
Theorem~\ref{thm:mnop-quintic} of that volume; Bridgeland's
Hall-algebra theorem; Behrend--Fantechi).

The formal-Darboux stalk supplies the tangent vector at one Dirac
brane vacuum: the local trace identification
$J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}\,f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-local} feeds the chain-level trace data on a
formal neighbourhood of a brane chart on $X$.  The global gluing
across $X$ requires a framing datum on the Lagrangian boundary;
for $L\cong S^3\hookrightarrow X$ the framing torsor is
$\pi_3(SO(3))=\Z$ (Costello brane-line BV framing;
\texttt{frontier\_mnop\_framing\_volume.tex},
Proposition~\ref{prop:s3-framing-quintic} of that volume).  The
Calabi--Yau volume form supplies the topological orientation and
trivialisation; choice of integer framing class
$[f]\in\pi_3(SO(3))=\Z$ together with the Costello-compatible
BV/$A_\infty$ chain homotopy $h_f$ trivialising the framing
obstruction class $\operatorname{Ob}_{\mathrm{frame}}(L,f)$ is the
global gluing data.  No global compact-CY$_3$ theorem is claimed
here: the construction problem is the matched-conventions firewall
of Theorem~\ref{thm:matched-conventions-transfer} restricted to
the GW/DT/PT/MNOP target with $S^3$-framing datum.

\subsection{Frontier input: Hall--Drinfeld realisation on compact
  CY$_3$}\label{ssec:frontier-hall-drinfeld}

The chiral specialisation
$\Phi_d=\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ch}}_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}\circ
\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d$ at $d=3$ realises a CY$_3$ category as a
chiral algebra on a curve through the two-stage functor of the
master architecture; the Hall--Drinfeld realisation
$\mathcal G(X)\simeq D(Y^+(X))$ on the spherical Hall double,
after Hall pairing, completion, integral form, stable-envelope
transport, and descent (Vol.~III, calabi-yau-quantum-groups), is
external comparison data, not load-bearing for the formal-Darboux
stalk theorem of this manuscript.  The matched-conventions class
$\operatorname{ob}_{\mathrm{VolIII}}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cross-volume-firewall} records the construction
data Vol.~III must supply for cross-volume transfer; the local
theorem implies the corresponding compact-CY$_3$ Vol.~III claim
only under joint vanishing of $\operatorname{Ob}_{\mathrm{UKD}}$.

\subsection{Frontier input: gravitational lift of
  \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_{10}$}{Phi10}}\label{ssec:frontier-phi10-gravitational}

The Igusa cusp form $\Phi_{10}=\chi_{10}\in
S_{10}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Z))$ is the level-$2$ section of
$\Omega_{\mathrm{central}}$ on $\overline{\mathcal A}_2$, realised
as the Gritsenko paramodular Borcherds product, with square-root
branch $\Delta_5=\Phi_{10}^{1/2}$.  Its conjectural gravitational
lift on $K3\times E$ (cross-volume reference: the Igusa cusp form
volume) is the construction of an operator-valued cocycle on the
chiral Hochschild bulk module whose protected scalar trace
recovers $\Phi_{10}$ at paramodular CM points
(Conjecture~\ref{conj:operator-modular-lift}).  The cross-volume
firewall of Theorem~\ref{thm:cross-volume-firewall} records the
matched-conventions class
$\operatorname{ob}_{\mathrm{Igusa/BKM}}$ which must vanish for the
lift to descend; external comparison data, not load-bearing for
the formal-Darboux stalk theorem.

\paragraph{The closing identification.}
The trace map
$J(f)=\overline{\operatorname{Tr}\,f(\phi_1,\phi_2)}$ is the formal
coordinate of the universal closed--open identification at one
Dirac brane vacuum; the global identification, the operator-level
lift of the modular line, and the chiral $E_d$ Deligne theorem at
$d\geq 2$ are the open problems the formal-Darboux stalk has now
made well-posed.

This work belongs to the lineage of Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa

Capelli--Howe scalar identities, Procesi--Razmyslov trace relations,
and Harish-Chandra radial-parts theorem supply the brane-probe side;
Matlis 1958 and Hartshorne local cohomology supply the principal-part
dual; Kontsevich's deformation quantisation supplies the all-order
Moyal target.
